# Deviations of the C ports from the originals

Complete overview of all known places where the
C versions (`libs/acidlibs/*.c`, `libs/deflibs/*`, `libs/userlibs/rwe/*.c` (the fixed original bugs), `programs/blitz2`, `programs/ted`) behave differently from the
originals (CD binaries or `blitz2.s`). Basis: all `DIFF` markers
in the sources, the DIFF sections in [cmdlibs-port.md](cmdlibs-port.md) and
[deflibs.md](deflibs.md), the project conventions and a review of our own ports
for unmarked deviations. `BUG(orig)` (original bugs not fixed yet) is
listed in the header comments and in `cmdlibs-port.md`, here only
where an entry describes it.

Rule (CLAUDE.md "Original bugs are fixed deliberately", user decisions
of 2026-10-30 and 2026-11-07): the port is complete, original bugs are
fixed deliberately, one issue per bug; every fix is listed here as an
intended deviation (kind **F**) with its issue and its test (a bug-orig
test with the header `; @fixed #N`, or the equivalent of its suite), in
the commit of the fix. Other deviations only where they are technically
unavoidable, with a reason. The deviations already present remain (not
reverted), but must be fully documented here.

## Categories

| Code | Kind | Meaning |
|---|---|---|
| **B** | user decision | project convention, fixed for all ports |
| **T** | technically unavoidable | the original is undefined here (register leftovers, stack contents, timing, memory layout) or cannot be expressed in C |
| **K** | silent correction | the original could be reproduced, the C version behaves "more safely" (trap instead of jump into nowhere, return instead of crash, register preserved). No longer permitted under the new rule; existing cases remain, but are listed here |
| **F** | fixed original bug | intended deviation after the port (CLAUDE.md "Original bugs are fixed deliberately": one issue per bug, in the default variant); the issue is named, and its test comes out FIXED: a bug-orig test with the header `; @fixed #N` (tests/README.md, "Fixed tests"), or the equivalent of its suite: the pair `KEY [orig]` / `KEY [fixed #NNN]` in the expected data of a library test (`libs/tests/expected`), `; @fixed #NNN PAIR...` in a `ted_ab` script, `; @fixed #N` in a TED bug-orig script, an expect file or override row with the issue number (tests/README.md, "Libraries", "TED A/B", "Corpus", "Oracle data of the TED tests"); an intended difference of a build check is listed with its issue in the `*-intended.txt` file of its area (docs/development.md, "Intended differences") |

"observable?": whether a Basic program can see the difference
(result, output, crash) or only a register/memory comparison.

## 1. Conventions (apply to all ports)

| Area | Original | C version | Kind | Reason / observable? |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| a2 (argument stack of the commands) | routines fetch lengths/types with `MOVE.x -(a2),dN` and leave a2 decremented by different amounts depending on the path | all routines get a2 as a `BL_REG(a2)` parameter, read via a local pointer (`BL_POPL`/`BL_POPW`), a2 stays unchanged | **B** | user decision; `preplibst` sets a2 anew before every call, the compiler never reads it afterwards (cmdlibs-port.md "General"). Not observable. Affects among others stringfunclib, vallib, printlib, editlib, intuifontlib, speaklib, audiolib, screenslib, gtlib, asllib, blitzcoplib, animlib, arexxlib, seriallib, fileiolib, brexxlib (Type) |
| Register preservation | a routine keeps what its code does not touch; the rule (LIBSDEV DOCUMENTATION:794-805, Mark Sibly): a routine keeps a4, a5 and a6 (and a7), a function also a3; a6 because the compiler keeps the library base it loaded across the calls of one statement (`lasta6`), a3 because it is the string build pointer of an expression | **user-level statements and functions** of the acidlibs and of the RWE userlib bb_blitlib (`docs/rwelibs/port/bb_blitlib.md`) are the C functions themselves (`BLITZ_ALIAS`, no thunk) and keep what the rule names (GCC also keeps d2-d7/a2), or assembler entries in the original's form where they are hot (Peek, Poke, Plot, Point, Joyx ..., RawStatus, Rnd, PColl, Int, Sgn, Abs ...: `cmdlibs-port.md`), which change only what the rule allows (Point d1-d3, Plot d2/d3 like the original, Rnd d0-d2); d0, d1, a0, a1 and the upper word of d0 after a word function are not kept, also where the original happens to keep them. **Exceptions** for known callers, each with a `KEEPS` case of exactly these registers (`systest_cmdlibs`): `RawStatus` a1 (RILesDebugLib `SearchMem`); `EventWindow` a1 (wzlib `WZGadHelp`, `WZMenuHelp`, `WZGadName`, `WZMenuName`); `EMouseX` a0, `EMouseY` a0 and the upper word of d0, `EventCode` a0 and the upper word of d0 (wzlib `WZInput`, `WZInputstr`, `WZGadHelp`, `WZGetNode`, `WZMenuHelp`: `EMouseX<<16\|EMouseY`; assembler entries `MOVE.w x,d0:RTS` as in the original); `InitSound` a0 (ElmoreIncLib `IncSound`, #1058); `Rnd` a0 and a1 (CD programs `SQUIRT2_TO.BB2`, `SQUIRT3_TO.BB2`, inline assembler); `WBWidth`, `WBHeight`, `WBDepth` a0 and a1 (CD program `WBDEMO.BB2`, inline assembler); `ExecVersion` returns a zero-extended long (ElmoreSysLib `checkrom` and ElmoreLib compare it as a long). Internal tokens keep their contract, not what the original happens to keep: those the compiler calls keep what its generated code holds across them (OpenSpec change `libs-performance`, design.md, Decision 17: operators and conversions d2-d7, string compare and copy d0-d7, the string assignment d1-d7 and a2, the procedure prologue routines d0-d5, freelast d0 and its flags), those third-party libraries call also what those callers hold across them (globalloc a0 and a1 and Z clear, globfree d1 and a0, allocst ($CF01) a0 and a1, freest d0, a0 and a1, fltoqu and qutofl a0 and a1, longmult a0 and a1); each with a `KEEPS` case that names the reason. Init and finit, nullsubs and every error check keep their thunks, which preserve at least what the original preserves | **B** | user decision 2026-11-15 (OpenSpec change `libs-performance`, register rule): the thunk cost 70 to 118 cycles per call. Observable only by inline assembler around a command and by an assembler library that calls the token and relies on a register the rule does not name; the callers known from the analysis of the CD's, AB2's and AB3's libraries and the CD's programs are the exceptions. A Basic program sees no difference |
| Error checks "at least the same" | check routines in the error check area | likewise (`BLITZ_ERRCHK`), messages as assembler data there | – | no deviation, except section 6 |

## 2. System libs

| Library/routine (file:line) | Original | C version | Kind | Reason / observable? |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| memlib err1 (memlib.c: `err1`, `localloc`) | jump out of localloc/newmem with saved registers on the stack, never returns | err1 returns, caller returns 0 | **K** | only if the runtime error trap returns (normally the program ends); practically not observable |
| memlib locfree err2 (memlib.c: `locfree`) | unknown block: one-time `TRAP #0` "Unable to Free Memory" (err2 in normal code); if the trap returns, the code continues in `newmem`, the registers saved by locfree are still on the stack | locfree returns after the runtime error | **K** | only with a returning trap; practically not observable |
| memlib messages (memlib.c:24) | err1/err2 trap in normal code, their messages "Out of Memory!"/"Unable to Free Memory" are in the error check area (without debugger d0 behind the lib) | messages in normal code; the error check area is empty | **F** | #956: the same runtime errors with the debugger; without it TRAP #0 with the right message (test `memlib-messages-errarea`) |
| memlib err1, "Out of Memory!" (memlib.c: `err1`) | frees every list header with its blocks (codefinit) before the runtime error: a program that catches it (SetErr) goes on without a list, and the next `localloc`/`globalloc` links its block into the list at address 0, writing the block's address over ExecBase at address 4 | every block is freed and the local lists with them, but the global list header stays, empty, as the only list (with its bucket; the buckets above it are freed), so a later allocation goes into it | **F** | #1071; observable only with SetErr: after a caught "Out of Memory!" the next allocation (test `memlib-err1-null-list`; 12 procedure levels deep: `regression/memlib-err1-null-list-deep`) |
| memlib list headers (memlib.c: `newmem`, `freelast`) | every procedure entry allocates its 8-byte list header with exec AllocMem, every exit frees it with FreeMem | the headers are entries of buckets of 8 (68 bytes, one AllocMem each), taken and given back in stack order; a bucket is freed when the one below it becomes empty too, the last one with the last list (program end, `$C006`) | **B** | user decision (OpenSpec change `memlib-header-buckets`): a procedure call without the exec pair for its header, about 1560 cycles less (`bproc.pstr`). Observable only as memory: one empty bucket (68 bytes, 72 with exec's rounding) may stay allocated while the program runs, so `AvailMem` after a deep recursion that came back can be up to 72 bytes lower than with the original; at the end everything is freed (tests `regression_memlib_free_*`) |
| arrayslib newlocarr/newloclist (arrayslib.c:29) | swaps a4/a5 around the memlib call | passes the base directly | **T** | a4/a5 are fixed registers in C; memlib does not use a5. Not observable |
| arrayslib newlist/newloclist (arrayslib.c:176) | links the first item before any check (do-while): a list of size 0 (e.g. `Dim List a(-1)`) gets its 8 bytes (next, last) written behind the 32 byte block | links an item only if its node fits; for the compiler's sizes (multiples of element size + 8) the same items | **F** | #59; observable only with size 0: no write behind the block (test `arrayslib-newlist-overrun`). AmiBlitz3 instead made size 0 a dynamic list (other list layout, not BB2-compatible) |
| arrayslib dimension calculation (arrayslib.c:90) | returns d1.w+1, changes d2 | d2 preserved, d1 undefined | **B** (register preservation) | compiler does not use d1/d2. Not observable |
| arrayslib arrmult (arrayslib.c: `arrmult_c`) | d0 = d0 * (d1.w+1) with two 16 bit MULUs; the second product is added with `SWAP` instead of shifted, and a size of 4 GB or more goes on modulo 2^32 (plus the SWAPped word): `Dim a.l(32767,32767)` gets 1 byte and succeeds | the second product shifted; a size that does not fit in 32 bits becomes $7FFFFFFF, whose allocation fails (memlib's runtime error "Out of Memory!"; no wrap in memlib's +8 or newlist's +32) | **F** | #58; observable: a `Dim` (or `Dim List`) of 4 GB or more is the runtime error "Out of Memory!" (test `arrayslib-arrmult-swap`); smaller sizes unchanged |
| arrayslib arrmult error check (arrayslib.c: `arrmultchk`) | no error check (arrayslib has no error check area): a size of 4 GB or more passes also with runtime error checking (`sarraychk` checks each dimension, not their product) | with runtime error checking the dimension that makes the size 4 GB or more is the runtime error "Array Size out of Range", before the allocation; release code unchanged ("Out of Memory!", row above) | **F** | #58; observable with runtime error checking (test `arrayslib-arrmult-swap`) |
| runerrlib array dimension check (runerrlib.c: `aarraychk_c`, `sarraychk_c`) | the error check of a dimension of Dim and Dim List ("Array Dimension out of Range") refuses only 65536 and more, but arrmult counts the elements as a word (highest index + 1): the highest index 65535 gives an array or list of 0 elements, and every access lies outside the block, also with runtime error checking | with runtime error checking the highest index 65535 is the runtime error "Array Dimension out of Range" too (an array has at most 65535 elements); release code unchanged (an invalid argument) | **F** | #1037; observable with runtime error checking (test `arrayslib-dim-65535`: 65534 passes, 65535 is refused) |
| maxlenlib (maxlenlib.c:19) | changes d2 and a2 | preserves d2/a2 | **B** | not results. Not observable |
| maxslib doallocst (maxslib.c:33) | d1 = -1 after copying | d1 preserved | **B** | not a result |
| maxslib dofreest (maxslib.c:55) | d0 = memlib's d0 | d0 preserved | **B** | not a result |
| modlib domodquick (modlib.c:29) | d1 returned swapped | d1 preserved | **B** | not a result |
| floatquicklib quicktofloat $D300 (floatquicklib.c:53, :68) | the sign is tested on the negated value (`NEG.l d0:BMI`): 0 stays 0 and takes the path of the negative numbers, so Quick 0 becomes the FFP "-0" `$00000080`; `NEG` of `$80000000` overflows and stays negative, so −32768.0 becomes +32768.0 | the sign is tested on the value (`TST.l`): Quick 0 gives `$00000000`, −32768.0 gives −32768.0 (`$800000D0`); one `TST.l` instead of the `NEG.l` of a copy, no extra work | **F** | #146, #147; observable: every Quick to Float conversion of 0 or −32768 (`Hex$(Peek.l(&f))`, `NPrint` of the Float) (tests `floatquicklib-zero-negative`, `floatquicklib-min-positive`) |
| ldivlib quickdiv $CB00, longdiv $CB01 (ldivlib.c:71, `.Linfinity`) | a divisor 0 is tested before the signs and gives `$7FFFFFFF` ("LARGEST POSITIVE NUMBER") for any dividend: −5/0 is positive | the largest number with the dividend's sign: `$7FFFFFFF` for a dividend ≥ 0, −`$7FFFFFFF` (`$80000001`) for a negative one; still no error. The zero test comes first at each assembler entry and costs a correct division one not taken BEQ.S, as before | **F** | #200; observable: every Long or Quick division of a negative number by 0 (test `ldivlib-div0`) |
| ldivlib error check (ldivlib.c: `divchk`) | quickdiv and longdiv have no error check (ldivlib has no error check area): a divisor 0 passes also with runtime error checking (the result of the row above), while a Word division by 0 is the debugger's "GURU! - Division by Zero" | with runtime error checking a divisor 0 executes `DIVU.W` with it, the CPU's division trap: the same "GURU! - Division by Zero" as for a Word division; release code unchanged (the result of the row above). The compiler never runs the check with a divisor 0 when it folds a constant division: it refuses a constant divisor 0 (#1044) | **F** | #200; observable with runtime error checking (tests `ldivlib-div0-check-long`, `-quick`; regression tests `ldivlib-div0-check-fold`, `-const`; corpus_runerr `t01_int`, whose `c/0` now ends the program with the requester): the executables with runtime error checking of the programs with a Long or Quick division change (the stub with the check); those without runtime error checking stay byte-identical, as tokecode builds no stub for the check there (#1048) |
| lmullib longmult $CA01, quickmult $CA00 (lmullib.c:60, :160) | the partial products of the magnitudes are added SWAPped instead of shifted by 16. Long: when a 16 bit cross product (hi·lo or lo·hi of the two magnitudes) is 65536 or more, its high word is also added to the low word of the result, whose magnitude is then too large by up to 65534 (`196608*30000` gave 1603272705). Quick: when the product of the integer parts is 65536 or more, the high word of that product is also added, up to 0.25 (`300*300` gave `$5F90.0001`) | the product modulo 2^32 (Quick: bits 16..47 of the 64 bit product of the magnitudes, the sign applied last); no call takes longer than the original's: longmult (assembler) needs no signs for the product modulo 2^32, multiplies by the words of \|b\| like the original and skips the partial products of zero high words, 50 to 222 cycles fewer per call; quickmult (assembler) multiplies by the same words of \|b\| as the original and keeps the sign in the code path instead of a flag register, 42 to 52 cycles fewer per call | **F** | #199; observable only for products that do not fit: a Long product changes only if a cross product is 65536 or more (the exact product of the magnitudes is then 2^32 or more), a Quick product only if the integer parts' product is 65536 or more; a product that fits is the same as before. The old results in Blitz code: `OldMul{}`/`OldQMul{}` in [bb2-readme.md](bb2-readme.md#lmullib-the-products-of-the-original-199) (test `lmullib-cross-swap`; libs_emulator `libtest_lmullib` BLOCK:1-11 and the `[fixed #199]` cases of `systest_mathstr`; corpus `t05_proc`, `t81_screen`) |
| VBR on the 68010: runerrlib codeinit (runerrlib.c: `codeinit_c`), environlib codeinit (environlib.c:105); the compiler's calcvbr in section 5 | the VBR is read (MOVEC) only if the highest AttnFlags CPU bit is 68020 or above: a 68010 gets VBR 0 (errtraplib puts its TRAP #0 handler, runerrlib's getvbr gives debuglib/rundebuglib, environlib's cr_vbr the table at 0, not the active one) | read from the 68010 on; errtraplib reads no VBR (its handler is the task's since #1069, row below) | **F** | #42: differs only on a 68010 with a moved vector table; test `multi-68010-vbr` (a500-68010: cr_vbr and a SetErr handler) |
| debuglib dout2 (debuglib.c: `dout2_c`) | the string output entry of the output handler that the debugger's evaluate installs (output of a string with a length, e.g. of a string variable) tests the length only for 0 and then copies until the 256 byte buffer is full: the debugger shows the memory behind a short string up to 256 characters | copies the given number of characters (at most up to the end of the buffer) | **F** | #126; observable with the debugger: the value of an evaluated expression. Test: snitch case `debuglib-dout2-length` (ctest `bug_orig_debuglib_dout2_length`, passes) |
| debuglib dofindoff $D507 (debuglib.c: `dofindoff_c`) | the conversion of a position in the tokenised line into the column of the detokenised text, with which the debugger marks the statement in the source, tests its end (`BEQ`) on the flags of the last instruction: after a token those of the length count, not of the position, which was lowered by 2 without a test; a position right after or inside a token ran on to the end of the line | the loop ends when the position reaches 0 or less, also after a token | **F** | #127; observable with the debugger: the column it marks after a token (e.g. after `Print`). Tests: snitch case `debuglib-findoff-flags` (ctest `bug_orig_debuglib_findoff_flags`, passes), the pairs `\|findoff\|N\|1 [orig]` / `[fixed #127]` of `systest_system` |
| rundebuglib printerr (rundebuglib.c:127) | the runtime error requester of an executable compiled with runtime error checking takes d0 of the TRAP #0 as the text pointer as it is, also for the numeric codes 1-3 that blitz2's code raises and only debuglib translates: 1 the used object test (`RGB` without a screen, a bitmap command without a used bitmap), 2 the bounds check of an array field of a NewType, 3 the end of a program without `End` (eopcode): the requester's text is empty (the byte at address 1-3) | the codes 1-3 get debuglib's texts "No currently used object!", "Offset out of range", "End of Program"; the stop at the end of a program without `End` stays (user decision 2026-11-09) | **F** | #6: only the text of these requesters; rundebuglib changes (linked into every executable compiled with runtime error checking). Corpus: `corpus_runerr` `t124_bitmaperr`, `t126_rgberr` now show "No currently used object!"; no source of the CD changes (`corpus_cd`). Test `rundebuglib-printerr-numeric-code` (the three codes). AB3 mode links AmiBlitz3's own rundebuglib (case `noend` of `tests/ab3`: still empty) |
| rundebuglib TRAP #0 in supervisor mode (rundebuglib.c: `more2`) | a runtime error in supervisor mode (e.g. in an interrupt routine) of an executable compiled with runtime error checking calls codefinit (old trap vectors back, CloseLibrary of intuition) and gives the TRAP #0 to the old vector with d0 changed by it: the old handler loses the message, intuition is closed in the supervisor context (and again by finit if the program goes on) | only the trap vectors the SetInt routine found are put back (#1069, row below); d0, the message, is kept for the old vector; intuition is closed by finit only; if the old handler returns, the routine is left through its end | **F** | #246; observable only by a TRAP #0 handler that was in the vector table before the program and reads d0 (test `rundebuglib-supertrap-d0-lost`: the message "Uninitialized Shape" instead of 0); rundebuglib changes (linked into every executable compiled with runtime error checking) |
| the trap handlers of runtime error checking and the debugger: rundebuglib, debuglib, errtraplib codeinit/codefinit (rundebuglib.c, debuglib.c, errtraplib.c: `codeinit`), runerrlib gosuper/gouser (runerrlib.c: `gosuper`, `gouser`) | rundebuglib and debuglib put their handlers into the CPU's vectors of TRAP #0-#5, errtraplib into that of TRAP #0, at the program's start, for the whole machine (rundebuglib and errtraplib put the old ones back at the end, debuglib never): with two such programs at once, the one that ends first puts back what it found while the other still runs, whose traps then reach the first one's handlers, those of a program that is gone, or exec's; a debugged program leaves its handlers in the table | each library makes its handler the program's task's trap handler (tc_TrapCode), which exec calls for a trap of that task in user mode; the CPU's vectors stay the system's. exec does not call it for a trap in supervisor mode, so a SetInt routine (supervisor mode) gets the CPU's TRAP #0-#5 vectors for the time it runs: runerrlib's gosuper (at its start, called before its TRAP #2 now, section 5) saves them on the stack and sets stubs that hand the trap to the task's handler as exec does, gouser (at its end) puts them back. debuglib takes the editor's token list from the task's tc_UserData (blitz2, section 5); debuglib and rundebuglib leave their handler to runerrlib's finit, as a SetInt routine runs until intlib's finit | **F** | #1069; observable: two programs with runtime error checking (or SetErr) at once work side by side (test `rundebuglib-two-programs`); runtime errors in a SetInt routine reach the debugger as before (regression test `debuglib-setint-runtime-error`). Changed: a trap in supervisor mode outside a SetInt routine (Blitz code with runtime error checking called through exec's Supervisor(), an interrupt server installed by hand) reaches exec, which stops the machine; the original's handlers took it. Every executable with runtime error checking and every one with errtraplib (SetErr) changes (the libraries' code); a SetInt routine compiled with runtime error checking costs about 20 moves more per interrupt (the six vectors saved, set and put back), release code is unchanged (libs_emulator: the `[fixed #1069]` cases of `systest_runtime` and `systest_system`) |
| runerrlib check chains (runerrlib.c:239) | continues through the error chain after a returning trap | C check returns | **K** | only with a returning trap (the debugger terminates). Practically not observable |
| datelib Date$ (datelib.c:268) | day 366 of a 4-year cycle (1 January after a leap year, e.g. `Date$(1096)`) stays in the leap year as "32/12" (`CMP #366`: `BLE`) | `BLT`: the 1 January of the next year | **F** | #120; observable: Date$, Days, Months, Years of these days (test `datelib-day366`). AmiBlitz3 rewrote the conversion as a year loop; here only the one compare, the date range and everything else stay |
| datelib Date$, NumDays (datelib.c:248, :320, :502) | 4-year cycles only: every year divisible by 4 is a leap year, also 1800, 1900, 2100, 2200, ...; the dates are right only from 1/3/1900 to 28/2/2100, outside a day off for each such century (`Date$(44619)` = 29/02/2100, `NumDays("1/1/9999")` = 2929670) | the day count of the cycles is corrected by the century leap days the Gregorian calendar does not have (a year divisible by 100 is a leap year only if divisible by 400): Gregorian dates, as those of AmigaDOS; WeekDay unchanged (it counts the days alone) | **F** | #1001; observable: Date$, Days, Months, Years and NumDays of dates before 1/3/1900 and from 1/3/2100 on (test `datelib-century-leap-years`, corpus `t30_date`); from 1/3/1900 to 28/2/2100 nothing changes |
| datelib WeekDay (datelib.c: `weekday_c`) | subtracts 2 from the day count with `SUBQ.w`: no borrow from the high word, so for a count whose low word is 0 or 1 (the days -1569/-1568, 15/16 September 1973, and 63967/63968 in 2153) the weekday is 2 too large | `SUBQ.l`; before the first Date$ the count is that of day 0 (WeekDay 0 there, as in the original) | **F** | #121; days before the year 0 (outside the range of Date$) give -1, as in the original, now also -722465 and -722464 (were 0 and 1); observable: WeekDay after Date$ of these days (test `datelib-weekday-borrow`, corpus `t30_date`: -1569 is a Saturday, 6, and 63967 a Monday, 1) |
| datelib NumDays (datelib.c: `calcdays_c`, the end) | corrects the day count with `ADDQ.w #1` (January and February of a year that is no leap year) and `SUBQ.w #1`: no carry or borrow into the high word, so `NumDays("31/12/1977")` is 65535, not -1, and `NumDays(Date$(65535))` 131071 | `ADDQ.l` / `SUBQ.l` | **F** | #122; observable: NumDays of a date whose count has the low word 0 before the `SUBQ` (the `ADDQ` case needs a year of 32297 or more, reachable only with characters other than 0-9) (test `datelib-numdays-word-carry`, corpus `t30_date`) |
| datelib NumDays month (datelib.c: `calcdays_c`) | indexes the table of month starts with the month without a check: month 0 reads the word before it (the tick word of the last DateStamp), 13 the end mark 1000, 14 and 15 the code at the end of the library, larger months foreign memory (`NumDays("1/13/1990")` = 5382) | a month outside 1-12 is invalid input: 0, as NumDays gives for a day or month not followed by "/" | **F** | #124; observable: NumDays of such a month, also with DateFormat 1 (test `datelib-numdays-month-range`, corpus `t30_date`) |
| datelib NumDays empty field (datelib.c: `calcdays_c`) | a "/" as the first character of a field ends it with the "/" itself, 47, as its value (`NumDays("/5/1990")` = 4549, the 47th of May); in the year field it jumps back and makes 47 the second field | an empty field is invalid input: 0 | **F** | #123; observable: NumDays of a date with an empty field (test `datelib-numdays-slash-field`, corpus `t30_date`) |
| datelib NumDays year (datelib.c: `calcdays_c`) | the year field ends only at a "/" or after 4 characters: a year of fewer than 4 digits without a "/" behind it (`"1/1/78"`) reads the 0 byte at the end of the string and the bytes behind it as further digits (character - 48), an empty year (`"1/1/"`) starts with them; the result depends on the memory behind the string | the year also ends at the end of the string (`"1/1/78"` is the year 78, as `"1/1/78/"`); an empty year is invalid input: 0 | **F** | #125; observable: NumDays with a short or empty year at the end of the string (test `datelib-numdays-short-year`). Two-digit years stay the years 0-99, as with "/" (the documentation names only dd/mm/yyyy) |
| datelib NumDays day and month (datelib.c: `calcdays_c`) | the day and month fields read their second character and the "/" behind it without a test for the end of the string: a short string (`""`, `"1"`, `"1/"`) reads the 0 byte as a digit and the bytes behind it, and parses on into the memory behind the string when a "/" follows (a date made of whatever is there) | a day or month field that ends at the end of the string is invalid input: 0 | **F** | #1029; observable: NumDays of a short string with a "/" behind its 0 byte in memory (test `datelib-numdays-short-field`) |
| fadelib calcfade (fadelib.c: `calcfade`) | every colour component is read with `MOVE.b` into d0 and the word shifted (`LSR #4,d0`) without clearing d0; d0.w comes in as the palette's object offset (palette# x 128): its bits 8-11 get into the red fraction, bits 12-15 into the green fraction of the first colour of the range, which jumps ahead in the steps of FadeIn/FadeOut of palette 2 and up | the components are shifted as bytes: the fractions are those of the palette alone; the fade set-up only (DoFade unchanged) | **F** | #134; observable: the first colour in the steps of a fade of palette 2 or up (test `fadelib-calcfade-palette-bits`: colour 0 rises 000 111 ... FFF; the original 100 311 ...) |
| fadelib palette check (fadelib.c: `fadechk0_x`) | the error check of FadeIn/FadeOut tests the palette with `TST (a0)`, the high word of its data pointer: a palette whose data lie in the lower 64 KB counts as not there ("Not enough colours in Slice", #330), a pointer with a non-zero high word passes | the data pointer is tested as a long; error check area only. The word test of the slice stays (+0 of a slice is the word y, not a pointer) | **F** | #135; observable with runtime error checking (test `fadelib-check-highword`: a palette data pointer of $8000 passes) |
| fadelib uninitialized palette message (fadelib.c: `fadechk0_x`) | the error check of FadeIn/FadeOut branches to `err2` for a palette without data: "Not enough colours in Slice"; `err3`/`mess3` "Uninitialized Palette" is never used | "Uninitialized Palette"; error check area only | **F** | #330; observable with runtime error checking (test `fadelib-uninit-palette-message`: FadeIn of a palette never created) |
| fadelib colour range message (fadelib.c: `fadechk_c`) | the error check of the form of FadeIn/FadeOut with low and high colour compares both with the colours of the slice but branches to `err1` when one is beyond them: "Uninitialized Slice" for a slice that exists | "Not enough colours in Slice" (`err2`); error check area only | **F** | #331; observable with runtime error checking (test `fadelib-colour-range-message`: FadeIn 0,1,0,8 on a slice of 8 colours) |
| fontlib messages (fontlib.c:47) | err1-err3 (Can't Open Font, Uninitialized BitMap/Font) trap in normal code, their messages are in the error check area (without debugger d0 behind the lib) | messages in normal code | **F** | #955: the same runtime errors with the debugger; without it TRAP #0 with the right message (test `fontlib-messages-errarea`) |
| fontlib bitchar (fontlib.c:377) | characters below tf_LoChar dropped with SUB.b/BMI (signed): also those ≥ LoChar+128 (topaz: every code from 160, the umlauts); with LoChar > 128 codes more than 128 below it drawn as c-LoChar+256 | unsigned compare: only characters below LoChar dropped | **F** | #148 (as AmiBlitz3 `99f38fed`, BMI → BCS); observable: such characters are drawn and move the cursor (test `fontlib-lochar-127`) |
| fontlib LoadBlitzFont (fontlib.c:41, doloadfont) | a font OpenDiskFont opens that is not 8x8 or is proportional (e.g. one diskfont scales to the 8 points asked for) is refused with "Can't Open Font" without CloseFont: it stays open after the program (tf_Accessors) | CloseFont before the runtime error | **F** | #150; observable as tf_Accessors of the font after the error (test `fontlib-loadblitzfont-not-closed`: diamond.font scaled to 8 points, caught with SetErr: 0; the original 1) |
| fontlib calcoff (fontlib.c:52) | after a trap (no bitmap/font) it runs into the next trap | draws nothing; unreferenced check `bitmapchk` missing | **K** | only with a returning trap |
| inputoutputlib defin single character (inputoutputlib.c:45, :233) | `BRA err1` into the error check area (without debugger: jump behind the lib) | trap and message in normal code | **K** | without debugger: runtime error instead of jump into nowhere (observable only in this error case). The jump would be technically possible (cf. audiolib, screenslib, shapeslib, whose error routines are in the normal code now, kind F) |
| inputoutputlib PopInput/PopOutput error check (inputoutputlib.c: `popinputcheck_c`, `popoutputcheck_c`) | the error check tests the push count for 0 (`TST ...count:BEQ`), but init's push of the initial handler counts too: the first PopInput/PopOutput right after the start passes the check although the program pushed nothing (and sets the handler to 0) | a count of 1 or less is the runtime error ("No Input channel to pop!" / "No Output channel to pop!"); release code unchanged | **F** | #1032; observable with runtime error checking: the error comes at the first pop without a push (corpus_runerr t44_popinerr: `t44_popinerr.fixed-1032.out`; t45_popouterr: the same output, the error one pop earlier) |
| inputoutputlib defin message (inputoutputlib.c: `mess1`) | the runtime error of a single character from the default input reads "Can't Input Character from Defualt Input" (a typo) | "Can't Input Character from Default Input" | **F** | #193; observable: the text of that runtime error, e.g. `Inkey$` without a window (test `inputoutputlib-default-typo`, corpus `t43_inkeyerr`) |
| wbstartuplib AddArg (wbstartuplib.c:77) | without memory, jump to `err` in the error check area, its message there too | runtime error with its message in normal code, NULL, caller aborts | **F** | #305 (the trap moved first as a silent correction, now also its message); only without memory at the start of the program, before any program code: no test (no handler of the program can see d0) |
| wbstartuplib Workbench start, dos.library (wbstartuplib.c: `codeinit_c`, `codefinit_c`) | init opens dos.library (`OldOpenLibrary`) for `CurrentDir` and never closes it: every Workbench start raises its open count for good | finit closes it (after restoring the current directory) | **F** | #306; observable: the open count of dos.library (test `wbstartuplib-dos-not-closed`, started by `wbrun`: +0) |
| wbstartuplib Workbench start, current directory (wbstartuplib.c: `codeinit_c`, `codefinit_c`) | `CurrentDir` to the program's lock drops the old current directory (its result), finit does not restore it: the process ends in the program's directory and the lock it started with is lost | init keeps the old current directory, finit makes it current again before it replies the WBStartup message | **F** | #307; observable: the current directory of the process at its end (test `wbstartuplib-currentdir`, started by `wbrun`) |
| wbstartuplib CLI string (wbstartuplib.c: `codeinit_c`) | started from the CLI, the argument line is copied into `cliString` (256 bytes) up to its 0 without a length check: a longer line overwrites what lies behind it | the line is cut to the 255 characters that fit (the arguments are taken from them); the byte behind them stays 0, so the scan behind a full line ends there | **F** | #308; observable: a CLI argument line of more than 255 characters (test `wbstartuplib-clistring-overflow`: a line of 306 characters gives an argument of 255) |
| linklistlib SortList (linklistlib.c:35) | destroys a3 and d6 | a3 unchanged | **K** | statement, a3 is not read afterwards. Not observable |
| linklistlib SortList keys (linklistlib.c: `dosortlist`) | compares two keys with `CMP.l` and `BMI`, the sign of their difference: for keys more than 2^31 apart the difference overflows (2000000000 sorts before -2000000000) | signed compare | **F** | #201; observable: SortList with keys more than 2^31 apart (test `linklistlib-sortlist-sign`, corpus `t31_list`) |
| memacclib Peeks$ (memacclib.c: `dopeeks_c`) | counts the length down unsigned (`SUBQ.l #1,d1:BNE`), only 0 is caught: a negative length copies up to 4 GB into the string buffer and overwrites the memory (crash) | a negative length gives the empty string (d0 = 0), as 0 does | **F** | #214; observable: Peeks$ with a negative length (test `memacclib-peeks-negative`); a positive length larger than the string buffer still overruns it (no check in either mode, as the original) |
| memacclib Peeks$ error check (memacclib.c: `peekschk`) | Peeks$ has no error check (memacclib has no error check area): a negative length passes also with runtime error checking | with runtime error checking a negative length is the runtime error "Can't Peeks$ a negative number of Characters!"; release code unchanged (the empty string of the row above) | **F** | #214; observable with runtime error checking (test `memacclib-peeks-negative-check`) |
| sortlib Sort of a string array (sortlib.c: `compare`) | an element that was never assigned (pointer 0) has its length read from -4(0) and its characters from 0 (with 24 bit addressing: the last long of the ROM as the length): the order is arbitrary | a pointer 0 is the empty string: it sorts first (SortUp) or last (SortDown) | **F** | #276; observable: Sort of a string array with never assigned elements (test `sortlib-string-null`, with 24 bit addressing and a "z" at address 0) |
| sortlib Sort of a word array (sortlib.c: `recurs`) | the pivot is read with `MOVE.l` and compared with `CMP.w`: its low word, the element after the middle; for a one element array (`Dim a.w(0)`) the word behind the array, and the scans read on behind it and possibly before it (reads only, the result is right) | the pivot is read as a word, the middle element; the sorted array is the same | **F** | #275; observable only as reads outside the array (snitch test `sortlib-word-pivot`, `[.bug-orig]`, ctest `bug_orig_sortlib_word_pivot`: Amiberry logs the reads behind an array at the end of the highest memory region; the original's scan runs on there until the session's time limit) |

## 3. Command libraries

| Library/routine (file:line) | Original | C version | Kind | Reason / observable? |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| literallib (literallib.c) | – (no library 33 in the CD's acidlibs) | a library of its own: number 33, 127 dummy tokens `$9081`-`$90FF` named by the characters `$81`-`$FF`, no code; behind the original's libraries in `acidlibs` of every variant without RWE, and as the userlib `literal/literallib.obj` for the original Blitz 2 | **F** | #1018 (an addition): TED shows a literal character `$90 cc` through the token list, a stray one is a Syntax Error; named as an addition in `declarations_acidlibs` (`libs/acidlibs/declarations-intended.txt`) and `tokens_port` (`tests/data/literallib-tokens.txt`); the first byte `$90` is reserved (`docs/token-table.md`) |
| 2dlib overflows (2dlib.c:57) | Polyf with more than 16 points overwrites the code behind polypts | overwrites the data behind it | **T** | memory layout; observable only with this overflow (an invalid argument, refused with runtime error checking since #395). FloodFill (yofill) is the original assembler, a3 as in the original |
| 2dlib drawpoly, polyf (2dlib.c:1581, :1692) | `Polyf` computes the outline of the polygon with word arithmetic: the slope of an edge is one `DIVS` of dx * 4^k by dy (it overflows from a dx of about 8192 on), an edge of 16384 lines or more gets the slope 0 (shift count -1), the clipping at the window edges overflows `DIVS` too, x positions are kept as twice x in a word, and the orientation test of the first three points takes `MULS` of the word differences (wrong sign from a difference of 32768 on): a polygon with vertices far outside the bitmap is drawn wrongly or not at all | a polygon of 3 to 16 points with an edge longer than 8191 in x or y or a point beyond +-16383 (after the bitmap origin) is first clipped to the bitmap plus one pixel with long arithmetic (Sutherland-Hodgman, the crossings rounded, MULU/DIVU), then drawn as before with edge lists of its own; the orientation test compares the exact products; the polygons within that range and those of fewer than 3 or more than 16 points (#395) are drawn as in the original | **F** | #1000: only polygons outside that range: they are drawn (a concave one, which Polyf does not support, as far as it gets; one whose clipping gives more than 20 points is not drawn); the clipping can put a vertical edge on x = -1 or x = width at the start of a side, which is not cut (#1033; the port regression #1034, test `regression/2dlib-polyf-clip-vertical-cut`); 2dlib changes. Corpus: `t158_2dfuzz` (both passes, the line `polyf wild`; the pair `t158_2dfuzz.orig.out` / `t158_2dfuzz.fixed-1000.out` in `tests/corpus/expect`). Test `2dlib-polyf-word-range` (triangles at +-20000 cover the bitmap in the right colour; a half triangle at +-20000 gives the pixels of the same at +-1000) |
| 2dlib side, polylib side (2dlib.c:1346, polylib.c:265; `roff`/`loff`) | the cut of a side where it leaves the clip width (the right side at x < 0, the left at x > width - 1) divides by the x difference of the edge without a test: when a side starts beyond that edge and its first edge is vertical (the top line spans the whole width, and the start handling moves in only one of the two starts), the difference is 0 and `Polyf`, `BitMapPoly`, `ChunkyPoly` and `RenderPoly` stop with "GURU! - Division by Zero" | a vertical edge beyond the clip width is not cut (it has no crossing); its lines stay in the edge list, and the drawing loop stops at the first of them, whose x is beyond the clip width, so the polygon is not drawn; the cut path costs 16 cycles more (CMP.W and BEQ.S, at most once per side) | **F** | #1033: only degenerate polygons (a side starting outside on a vertical edge; simple rectangles, triangles and convex shapes wider than the bitmap do not reach it); 2dlib and polylib change. With the clipping of #1000 also polygons outside the word range reach it (#1034: the port regression, the original survives them). Tests `2dlib-polyf-vertical-cut`, `polylib-vertical-cut` ("before", "after" instead of the guru), `regression/2dlib-polyf-clip-vertical-cut` (#1034) |
| 2dlib floodfill2 (2dlib.c: `floodfill2`, `invertmask`) | `FloodFill x,y,colour,border` inverts the mask of the border colour with one blit that uses the pointer and BLTSIZE `monofetch` leaves behind, those of its last column of 64 words (and of the last part of height MOD 1024 lines): on a bitmap wider than 1024 pixels (or taller than 1024 lines) only that part is inverted and the fill goes wrong | the mask is inverted in the columns of 64 words and the parts of 1024 lines `monofetch` fetched it in (`invertmask`); a bitmap of at most 1024 x 1024 pixels gets the one blit of the original. Once per FloodFill, not in the fill loop | **F** | #986; observable: FloodFill with a border colour on a bitmap wider than 1024 pixels or taller than 1024 lines (test `2dlib-floodfill-border-wide`: boxes on 1280 x 100 and 64 x 1100 bitmaps, which the original leaves unfilled; corpus `t154_fill`, the lines from `wide border` on: the pair `t154_fill.orig.out` / `t154_fill.fixed-986.out` in `tests/corpus/expect/corpus` and `corpus_runerr`) |
| 2dlib cplot, cplot2 (2dlib.c: Circle, Circlef and the ellipse forms) | the CPU plot compares y with the clip height signed (`CMP.w 58(a3),d1:BPL`) and then multiplies its unsigned word with the bytes per row: a point above the top edge (y = -1 = 65535) is set up to 65535 rows behind each plane (Circle) or toggled behind the fill buffer (Circlef) | the compare is unsigned (`BCC`): a point above the top edge is clipped like one below the bottom edge; the same instructions, no cycle more per point | **F** | #392; observable: Circle or Circlef reaching above the top edge, after the bitmap origin (test `2dlib-circle-negative-y`) |
| 2dlib AllocFill (2dlib.c: declaration, `_allocfill`) | the statement is declared without `!libs` (no used bitmap), but the routine reads the clip width and height of the bitmap at a3: called from BASIC, a3 is the string work pointer, and the fill buffer gets a size from two words of the string buffer (nothing, or up to about 512 MB, which fails) | declared with the used bitmap (`!libs {#bitmaplib,#ua3}`): the buffer is sized for the used bitmap; the routine is the same | **F** | #390; observable: AllocFill as a BASIC statement (test `2dlib-allocfill-a3`); the declaration differs from the CD's (`libs/acidlibs/declarations-intended.txt`) |
| 2dlib Box (2dlib.c: `box`) | adds the bitmap origin (BitMapOrigin) to both corners and then draws the sides with the line clipper, which adds it again: the outline is displaced by twice the origin (Line, Boxf and Plot by the origin once) | only the line clipper adds the origin: Box lies where Line and Boxf put it; 4 ADDs fewer per Box | **F** | #391; observable: Box after BitMapOrigin with x or y not 0 (test `2dlib-box-origin-twice`; corpus `t151_line`, the line `origin`: the pair `t151_line.orig.out` / `t151_line.fixed-391.out` in `tests/corpus/expect/corpus` and `corpus_runerr`). A program that draws Box with half the origin to make up for the doubling now draws it displaced |
| 2dlib Poly error check (2dlib.c: `_polychk`) | Poly has no error check (2dlib has no error check area): with fewer than 2 points the word counter runs from 0 (or -1) down through $FFFF, and 65535 or 65536 lines are drawn from the words behind the coordinates, also with runtime error checking | with runtime error checking fewer than 2 points are the runtime error "Poly needs at least 2 Points"; release code unchanged (an invalid argument) | **F** | #396; observable with runtime error checking (test `2dlib-poly-one-point`) |
| 2dlib, polylib edge lists (2dlib.c, polylib.c: `pv.ledges`, `pv.redges`) | the edge lists of a side (`l0`, `l1`: `Dcb.w 32`, 3 words per edge and the end mark) have room for 10 edges, and the side walk writes without a limit: a polygon of up to 16 points with more than 10 edges on one side (Polyf, BitMapPoly, ChunkyPoly, RenderPoly) writes the left list into the right one, which then overwrites it, or behind the right one, and is drawn wrongly | the lists have room for 15 edges, as many as a side of a polygon of 16 points has; the code is the same | **F** | #395; observable: a polygon with 11 to 15 edges on one side (tests `2dlib-polyf-overflow`, `polylib-bitmappoly-edge-overflow`) |
| 2dlib Polyf, polylib ChunkyPoly, BitMapPoly, RenderPoly error checks (2dlib.c: `_polyfchk`; polylib.c: `_polychk`) | no error check (neither library has an error check area; ChunkyPoly and RenderPoly have only "Blitz mode only"): more than 16 points overflow the point buffer `polypts`, the original's own code behind it (a guru), also with runtime error checking | with runtime error checking more than 16 points (for the reverse form of polylib: a count below -16) are the runtime error "Too Many Points"; release code unchanged (an invalid argument) | **F** | #395; observable with runtime error checking (tests `2dlib-polyf-points-check`, `polylib-bitmappoly-points-check`) |
| polylib ChunkyVlin error check (polylib.c: `_chunkyvlinchk`) | `ChunkyVlin x,y1,y2,colour` without CLIP counts y2 - y1 with DBRA and does not swap the ends (the CLIP form does): with y2 < y1 about 65536 words are written, one per chunky line, also with runtime error checking (no check) | with runtime error checking y1 greater than y2 in the form without CLIP is the runtime error "ChunkyVlin y1 MUST NOT be greater than y2"; release code unchanged (an invalid argument; the plain form is the fast one) | **F** | #417; observable with runtime error checking (test `polylib-chunkyvlin-reverse`) |
| polylib RenderPoly before RenderTo... (polylib.c: `polyvector`, `render_none`) | `polyvector` starts at `_bitmappoly`: RenderPoly (declared without `!libs`) before any RenderToBitmap or RenderToChunky draws the polygon on a3 as the bitmap, called from BASIC the write pointer of the string work buffer | `polyvector` starts at a routine that draws nothing; the first RenderTo... sets it as before | **F** | #420 (the issue's own fix); observable: RenderPoly without a RenderTo... before it (test `polylib-renderpoly-a3`) |
| polylib RenderPoly colour error check (polylib.c: `_renderpolychk`) | RenderPoly after RenderToChunky takes the colour through the table of 15 chunky colours without a check (RenderPoly has only "Blitz mode only"): a colour above 14 reads the words behind the table, also with runtime error checking | with runtime error checking a colour above 14 after RenderToChunky is the runtime error "Illegal Colour"; release code unchanged (an invalid argument). The check of RenderPoly also counts the points (#395) | **F** | #422; observable with runtime error checking (test `polylib-renderpoly-colour-check`) |
| polylib RenderPoly after RenderToBitmap b,d (polylib.c: `render_dither`) | `RenderToBitmap BitMap#,DitherBitMap#` stores the two bitmaps, but RenderPoly (`rpoly1`) loads them swapped: it draws on the dither bitmap with the target as the dither pattern, and the target stays unchanged | draws on BitMap# with DitherBitMap# as the pattern, as the declaration says; the same instructions | **F** | #421; observable: RenderPoly after the two-bitmap form of RenderToBitmap (test `polylib-rendertobitmap-swapped`; corpus `t156_bmpoly`, the lines `render` and `render 2`, and `t159_polyfuzz`, the line `render dither`: their `[fixed]` files rewritten). A program that swapped the arguments to make up for the bug now draws on the other bitmap |
| polylib overflows (polylib.c:40) | more than 16 points hit the lib's own code | hit the data behind them | **T** | memory layout; only with this overflow (an invalid argument, refused with runtime error checking since #395) |
| polylib _chunkyvlinclip (polylib.c: `_chunkyvlinclip`) | `ChunkyVlin x,y1,y2,colour,CLIP` rejects a negative x and clips y against the chunky height, but does not compare x with the width (`ChunkyPlot` with CLIP does): the line is drawn at `(x AND 31) * 4 + (x / 32) * 132` from the start of the chunky area, into other rows or behind the area | an x at or beyond the chunky width is clipped away as in `ChunkyPlot` with CLIP: one compare more per call of the CLIP form (about 12 cycles), nothing per pixel | **F** | #987; observable: `ChunkyVlin` with CLIP and an x at or beyond the chunky width (test `polylib-chunkyvlin-clip-x`: on a 64 pixel wide chunky area pointed at a zeroed block, `ChunkyVlin 100,0,15,$FFF,CLIP` wrote 16 words, now none; corpus `t159_polyfuzz`, the line `vlin`: the pair `t159_polyfuzz.orig.out` / `t159_polyfuzz.fixed-987.out` in `tests/corpus/expect/corpus` and `corpus_runerr`) |
| polylib dither form (polylib.c: `bitmappoly_dither`) | the B modulo of the one blit per line over all planes is mod + (target plane distance - dither plane distance): B advances per plane by 2 x the target distance - the dither distance instead of the dither bitmap's own plane distance; with a dither bitmap of smaller planes than the target, planes 1 and up read behind the dither bitmap (a pattern that depends on that memory) | B advances by the dither bitmap's plane distance (the difference computed once per call, nothing per line or pixel) | **F** | #1035; observable: BitMapPoly or RenderPoly with a dither bitmap whose planes differ in size from the target's (test `polylib-dither-plane-step`) |
| polylib word range (polylib.c: `side`, `outline`, `muldiv`, `edge_start`) | BitMapPoly, ChunkyPoly and RenderPoly compute edge slopes, clip crossings and coordinate differences in words: an edge of 4096 lines or more that is at least as wide as high is drawn vertical (the slope overflows `DIVS`), and two neighbouring points 32768 or more apart wrap in the clipping, so the polygon is not drawn | the same (reproduced) | – | #1019: original bug carried over, **won't fix** (a range check on every call costs speed in a rendering library; a program clips its polygons before the call; test `polylib-word-range`: the triangle (0,0), (8192,4096), (0,4096) on a 320x200 bitmap draws 4880 bytes fewer than the same triangle with s = 200 in the CD's library and the C library, the correct result is 0 differing bytes) |
| animlib err6/err7 (animlib.c:46) | Can't Open Anim File: `BEQ err7` into the error check area; err6 (Anim format not supported) traps in normal code with its message in the error check area and runs on into err1 if the trap returns | traps and messages in normal code; LoadAnim returns if the trap returns | **F** | #398: the same runtime errors with the debugger; without it TRAP #0 with the right message (test `animlib-errors-check-area`) |
| animlib NextFrame (animlib.c: `donextframe_c`) | after the last delta the wrap takes the second node: with a single DLTA that is 0, the current delta becomes 0, and the next NextFrame reads its delta node at address 0 (also the first NextFrame of an anim without ANHD or without DLTA); with the debugger `animchk3` stops it ("Anim has only 1 frame") | the wrap takes the first node when there is no second one (the anim plays once and stays on its last frame); without a current delta NextFrame does nothing; `animchk3` unchanged | **F** | #401; observable without the debugger: an ANIM with one DLTA or none (tests `animlib-single-delta`, `animlib-no-delta`) |
| animlib LoadAnim without BODY (animlib.c:339, doanimload) | the result of ifffind("BODY") is stored as the BODY length without testing "not found": iffiolib's -1 becomes the length, memlib allocates 7 bytes plus its header, the read copies nothing and InitAnim decodes whatever memory follows the block into the bitmap | a missing BODY is no BODY: length 0, nothing allocated or read; InitAnim decodes nothing (it still sets the current delta) | **F** | #400; observable: the BODY length of the anim object (+14) after LoadAnim of a file without BODY (test `animlib-missing-body`) |
| animlib dimension 0 (animlib.c:343, doanimload; initanim, doinitanim2, donextframe) | InitAnim, its second form and NextFrame count rows, planes and columns with SUBQ #1 ... DBF: a BMHD with height, depth or width 0 runs a loop 65536 times (with depth 0 over 65536 plane pointers behind the bitmap, with height 0 over 65536 rows of the BODY) | LoadAnim keeps no BODY and no deltas of a picture with a dimension 0 (file data: refused in release code, no error); InitAnim decodes nothing, its second form copies nothing, NextFrame has no delta | **F** | #402; observable: the BODY length and the deltas after LoadAnim (tests `animlib-zero-dimensions`: height 0, the plane stays 0; `animlib-zero-dimensions-load`: depth 0, width 0) |
| animlib InitAnim BODY (animlib.c:448, initanim) | the BODY is decoded as ByteRun1 whatever the BMHD's compression byte says (LoadAnim does not keep it), and a run longer than the rest of the row is written behind the row: an ANIM with an uncompressed first frame gives a wrong picture and runs of up to 128 bytes behind the rows and the last plane | LoadAnim marks a BODY of compression 0 with 1 in the byte +30 (the upper byte of the EOR word, 0 in the original and still 0 for ByteRun1; the EOR flag is the byte +31); InitAnim copies stored rows, and ends a ByteRun1 run at the end of the row (the rest of it dropped) | **F** | #397; observable: the bitmap after InitAnim of an uncompressed ANIM or one with an over-long run, `Peek.w(Addr Anim(n)+30)` of an uncompressed ANIM is 256 (test `animlib-body-always-byterun1`) |
| animlib freeanim (animlib.c:276) | the walk over the delta nodes (Free Anim, LoadAnim over a loaded anim, the program end) reads the next pointer from a node after freeing it (memlib globfree, exec FreeMem): a use after free, harmless only while nothing reuses the block | the next pointer and the length of the next node are read before the node is freed | **F** | #399; observable with a memory checker or another task reusing the block (test `animlib-freeanim-after-free`: inject `freefill=FF` sets a freed block to $FF, the original then follows $FFFFFFFF) |

| medlib finetune (medlib.c: `f_15` of `medlib_newline`, `InitModule`) | the finetune of the InstrExt or of the command 15xx (range -8..7, checked only with `#CHECK = 1`) indexes the 16 period table pointers times 4 without a check: a value outside the range reads a pointer before or behind the table (another table, the chip zero buffer or any memory) as the period table of the note | 15xx with a value outside -8..7 is ignored (the original's `#CHECK` code); an InstrExt value outside it becomes 0 when the module is started. | medlib Bxx position jump (medlib.c: `f_0b` of `medlib_newline`) | stores the word of d4 as the position; d4 is cleared once per line and loaded bytewise per track, so its upper byte is left from a track before in the same line (19xx, the soffset or 64 that `_PlayNote` leaves): the position is 256 or more and the player takes position 0 and repeats it, or the word is negative and the block number is read before the play sequence | stores the byte xx (zero-extended) | **F** | #203; only lines with Bxx behind such a track; costs two instructions (8 cycles) more on a Bxx line only (test `medlib-bxx-word`: 19 08 in track 0, 0B 05 in track 1, the jump goes to position 5) |
| medlib 1Dxx pattern break (medlib.c: `f_1d` of `medlib_newline`) | adds 1 to the word of d4 and stores it as the next line; d4 is cleared once per line and loaded bytewise per track, so its upper byte is left from a track before in the same line (19xx, the soffset or 64 that `_PlayNote` leaves): the line is 256 or more, it is kept at the change of the block (nxtnoclrln), and the notes are read from the memory behind the block | takes the byte xx (zero-extended) + 1 | **F** | #1066; only lines with 1Dxx behind such a track; costs two instructions (8 cycles) more on a 1Dxx line only (test `medlib-1dxx-word`: 19 08 in track 0, 1D 01 in track 1, the next line is 1) |

| iffmakelib makebody (iffmakelib.c:312) | the BMHD's nPlanes is the byte Depth at BitMap+5, but the plane loop of the BODY compares with the word at +4, Flags * 256 + Depth: a bitmap whose Flags byte is not 0 gets Flags * 256 + Depth planes per row (plane pointers read behind the BitMap, an invalid file) | compares with the byte Depth | **F** | #180: only `SaveBitmap` of a bitmap whose Flags byte is not 0 (`SaveScreen` clears it in its copy, `SaveShape` writes the depth as a word); test `iffmakelib-plane-word` |
| iffmakelib err2 (iffmakelib.c:49) | "Can't open File" (err2) traps in normal code, its message is in the error check area (without debugger d0 behind the lib) | message in normal code | **F** | #185: the same runtime error with the debugger; without it TRAP #0 with the right message (test `iffmakelib-openerr-errarea`) |
| menuslib MenuChecked of a subitem (menuslib.c: `domenuchecked2_c`) | walks to the subitem with three `DBF` loops, but the item and the subitem loop jump back to the menu loop: only (menu,0,0) ends at the right entry; any other item or subitem walks the lists again with a counter of -1 (up to 65536 next pointers, from an item into whatever follows): the state of another entry, or reads of foreign memory | the three loops of `MenuState` with a subitem (`domenustate4`): the state of the subitem asked for | **F** | #217; observable: `MenuChecked(l,m,i,s)` with i or s not 0 (test `menuslib-menuchecked-sub-loops`: the original gives 0 for a checked subitem 0 of item 1) |
| menuslib SubItem replacing a subitem (menuslib.c: `subitem`, `finditem`) | `findthing` moves the entries behind a replaced one by (d3 − old height), d3 meant as the new height; for `SubItem` the `MOVEM` after `textcalc` restores d3, the subitem number: the following subitems move by (number − old height), overlapping it or leaving a gap | the new height is passed, as for `MenuItem`: the following subitems move by the height difference | **F** | #220; observable: the TopEdge of the subitems behind a replaced one (test `menuslib-subitem-replace-shift`; corpus `t73_menus`, its `[fixed #219]` output: "Sub two long" at 14 instead of 2) |
| menuslib sussflags (menuslib.c: `sussflags`) | the Flags argument is tested as a long for 0 (`MOVE.l -(a2),d7:BEQ`), but compared with 5 as a word (`CMP #5,d7:BCC`): a value with a high word and a low word below 5 (`$10000`) takes the branch of 4 (checked toggle, all others excluded) | the long is compared: `$10000` is "the flags themselves", its low word 0 | **F** | #219; observable: the Flags and MutualExclude of items made with such a value (corpus `t73_menus`, `MenuItem 0,$10000,...`: `$0002`/0 instead of `$015B`/`$FFFFFF7F`; pair `[fixed #219]`) |
| menuslib ShapeItem, ShapeSub flags (menuslib.c: `shapecalc`, `shapecont`, the thunks of `doshapeitem`/`doshapesub`) | the Flags argument (a pushed long) is fetched with `MOVE 4(a7),d7`, its high word, and read by `sussflags` as a long behind the cleared low word of `mexclude`: flag values below $10000 (1-4: toggle, exclude, checked) give the default flags ($50), larger ones are taken as value >> 16; the item Flags are set with `MOVE` (the same as the `OR` of `menucont` for the new entry) | the whole long, as `MenuItem`: the same flags as a text item, without `ITEMTEXT`; ORed as in `menucont` | **F** | #218; observable: the Flags and MutualExclude of shape items and subitems with flags (test `menuslib-shapeitem-flags-hiword`) |
| menuslib MenuState of a menu (menuslib.c: `domenustate2_c`) | `MenuState MenuList#,Menu,On\|Off` goes through `changestate2`, which is meant for items, and sets or clears bit 4 of the Menu's Flags instead of `MENUENABLED` (bit 0): a single menu cannot be disabled or enabled | sets or clears `MENUENABLED`, as `MenuState MenuList#,On\|Off` does for the whole list | **F** | #216; observable: the Flags of the menu and whether Intuition shows it disabled (test `menuslib-menustate-menu-bit`) |
| menuslib textcalc, MenuTitle (menuslib.c: `textcalc`, `domenutitle`) | the text of an item or subitem is copied with `allocst`, which returns 0 for a text of length 0; the 0 becomes the IntuiText's `IText` and `IntuiTextLength` is called on it: it reads the string at address 0 (Enforcer hit) and the item's width is that of whatever is there (with "ABC" at address 0: 32 instead of the 8 of the gaps alone). `MenuTitle` with an empty title also stores `MenuName` 0 (Intuition reads the title there), but computes its width from the length alone | an empty text is copied with length 1 (its 0 byte): `IText` and `MenuName` are the empty string, the item has the width of the gaps alone; the title's width is unchanged | **F** | #981; observable: the width of an item or subitem with an empty text, the Enforcer hit (test `menuslib-empty-item-text`). AmiBlitz 2.42 lists the fix ("fixed harmless enforcer hit on menutitles with string """), AmiBlitz3's first commit gives an empty item text the length 1, as here |

## 3a. RWE userlibs (`libs/userlibs/rwe`)

The other deviations of these ports are in their notes
(`docs/rwelibs/port/NAME.md`, DIFF); the fixed original bugs are here.

| Library/routine (file:line) | Original | C version | Kind | Reason / observable? |
|---|---|---|---|---|

| neilsreqtoolslib RTEZMultiLoadFile (neilsreqtoolslib.c:1111) | tests the file list address with `CMP.b #0,d0`: a list at an address ending in `$00` is returned as its address instead of -1 | -1 for every list, 0 on cancel | **F** | #462: only a list at an address ending in `$00` (about one in 32), with a program that compares the result with -1 (`libs_emulator` cases `[fixed #462]` of `rwe_neilsreqtoolslib`, fake reqtools.library) |

| neilsreqtoolslib RTEZFlagsRequest (neilsreqtoolslib.c:880) | stores the title in RTEZRequest's tag list: its own has `RTEZ_ReqTitle, 0`, the requester always shows reqtools' default title | the title in its own list, an empty title as 0 (the default title) | **F** | #455: every RTEZFlagsRequest with a title (`libs_emulator` cases `[fixed #455]` of `rwe_neilsreqtoolslib`, fake reqtools.library: the tag list passed) |

| neilsreqtoolslib init, finit (neilsreqtoolslib.c:672) | when an rtAllocRequestA fails, the library stays open and the requesters allocated so far stay allocated (finit does nothing then); finit clears nothing | init frees the requesters it has and closes the library (IsReqToolsActive 0, d0 0, D-018); finit clears the pointers, the base and IsReqToolsActive | **F** | #460: only with too little memory at program start (`libs_emulator` cases `[fixed #460]` of `rwe_neilsreqtoolslib`, fake reqtools.library: the calls of the failed inits, the library's open count) |

| riamosfunclib EraseAll (riamosfunclib.c: `eraseall_c`; also finit, BankLimit) | counts from maxbanks − 1 down with `DBRA`, which tests the low word only, while the bank number is the whole long: with more than 65536 banks only those down to the next multiple of 65536 are freed (bank 0 … 65535 stay allocated), `BankLimit 0` runs through 65536 negative banks before the tables (a crash) | counts the whole long down to 0; with 0 banks nothing | **F** | #495: only `BankLimit 0` or more than 65536 banks (test `riamosfunclib-eraseall-dbra`) |

| rievallib ConvToPostFix `-` after a space (rievallib.c: `ctp_loop`) | takes a `-` as a sign only if the character right before it is an operator or `(` (or it starts the string): behind a space it is a binary minus without an operand, `4* -2` becomes `4 * 2 - ` and PFEvaluate fails with error 1 | skips the spaces in front of the `-` first: a sign behind an operator, `(` or only spaces (`4 -2 * `), a binary minus behind an operand (`3 -4` stays 3-4) | **F** | #633: only a negative number written with a space between it and the operator, `(` or the start of the string (test `rievallib-ctp-sign-after-space`, `libs_emulator` case `[fixed #633]` of `rwe_rievallib`) |
| rievallib ConvToPostFix `.` at the end (rievallib.c: `ctp_loop`) | copies a `.` inside an operand with the next character, unchecked: at the end of the string that is its 0, written into the postfix buffer, and the conversion goes on with the bytes behind the string up to the next 0 (the buffer may overflow by their conversion) | copies the character behind the `.`, but not the 0: a `.` at the end ends the operand (`1+2.` gives `1 2. + `) | **F** | #631: only a string that ends in `.` (a typo such as `{$100}.` or `9.`) (test `rievallib-ctp-dot-at-end`, `libs_emulator` cases `[fixed #631]` of `rwe_rievallib`) |
| rievallib PFEvaluate `*`, `^` (rievallib.c: `lmult`) | adds the cross products after a `SWAP` with their high word in the low word: a product that does not fit in 32 bits is not its low 32 bits (`1000000 1000000 *` is -727379962) | the cross products' low words into the high word only: the low 32 bits of the product (-727379968), as for every product that fits | **F** | #639: only products (also inside `^`) whose cross products exceed 16 bits, i.e. that overflow 32 bits (test `rievallib-lmult-overflow`, `libs_emulator` case `[fixed #639]` of `rwe_rievallib`) |
| rievallib PFEvaluate `{register}` (rievallib.c: `ev_loop` `getreg`, `ev_indirect`) | checks the `}` of an indirection in d1, the last character its number loops read: a register operand (`{d0}`) does not go through them, d1 is stale (the caller's, or what the last number, `*`, `/` or `^` left) and the check mostly fails with error 5 "No close bracket on indirect!"; if d1 happens to be `}`, the `}` still in the string is then read as a value 0 | the register path checks and consumes the `}` at the string: `{d0}` is the long at the address in d0 | **F** | #637: every register inside `{...}` (RWE's debugger evaluates such typed expressions) (test `rievallib-indirect-register-stale-d1`, `libs_emulator` cases `[fixed #637]` of `rwe_rievallib`) |
| rievallib PFEvaluate `{address}` (rievallib.c: `ev_indirect`) | always reads a long at the address (`MOVE.l (a0),d0`) and takes its top byte for `.b`, its top word for `.w`: at an odd address an address error on a 68000/68010 (a crash), on every processor a read of 3 bytes more than `.b` asks for | reads the size first, then that many bytes one by one (the same values) | **F** | #636: only an indirection at an odd address on a 68000/68010 (test `rievallib-indirect-odd-long`, on the A600; the emulated 68020 of `libs_emulator` raises no address error, its cases are unchanged) |
| rievallib PFEvaluate digits (rievallib.c: `ev_loop`, the number loops) | takes every character of a number up to the next space (or `}`) as a digit, unchecked: decimal adds c-`0`, hex c-`0` or c-`a`+10, binary ORs c-`0` (`12x` is 192, `%2` 2, `$g` 16, `1.5` 2645, `$FF` 374), without an error | a character that is no digit of the base (0-9; `$` 0-9 and a-f, lower case as ConvToPostFix writes it; `%` 0 and 1) is error 4 "No result value!" with the result 0 (no new error number: user decision W2 Q1) | **F** | #634: only numbers with a character that is no digit (also an upper-case hex digit, a `(` or a `{` behind a `-` in a string not written by ConvToPostFix) (test `rievallib-digits-unchecked`, `libs_emulator` cases `[fixed #634]` of `rwe_rievallib`) |
| rievallib PFEvaluate register operands (rievallib.c: `ev_loop` `evall`, `getreg`) | takes the character behind `a`/`d` minus `0` (plus 8 for `a`) as the index into the PFRegisters block, unchecked, and skips it: `d9` reads index 9, `dx` index 72, a final `d` takes the string's 0 (index 208) and goes on with the bytes behind the string; a character behind a register starts the next operand (`d01` is d0 and 1) | `a0`-`a7`, `d0`-`d7` and `pc`, followed by a space, the end or (in `{...}`) its `}`; any other is error 4 "No result value!" with the result 0 (no new error number: user decision W2 Q1) | **F** | #642: only register operands with another index character or a character behind them (test `rievallib-register-index-unchecked`, `libs_emulator` cases `[fixed #642]` of `rwe_rievallib`) |
| rievallib PFEvaluate values left (rievallib.c: `ev_loop`, the end of the string) | returns the top value at the end of the string without checking that it is the only one: `1 2` is 2, `1 2 + 3` 3, without an error | more than one value left is error 4 "No result value!" with the result 0 (no new error number: user decision W2 Q1) | **F** | #635: only an expression with too few operators (also operands that ConvToPostFix split) (test `rievallib-extra-values-no-error`, `libs_emulator` cases `[fixed #635]` of `rwe_rievallib`) |
| rievallib ConvToPostFix other characters (rievallib.c: `ctp_loop`) | ends an operand at a character that is no digit, a-f, register, `$ % . { }` and drops it, without an error: `1,2` becomes `1 2 `, `12x+3` `12 3 + ` (PFEvaluate: 15) | keeps the character in the operand (`1,2 `, `12x 3 + `); PFEvaluate rejects it with error 4 "No result value!" (#634, #642); ConvToPostFix itself has no error result | **F** | #632: only expressions with a character that is no part of a number, register, operator or bracket (test `rievallib-ctp-drops-characters`, `libs_emulator` cases `[fixed #632]` of `rwe_rievallib`) |
| rievallib PFEvaluate `^` (rievallib.c: `ev_loop`) | returns 1 for the base 0 (`0 5 ^`), the base itself for the exponents 1, 0 and every negative one (`3 0 ^` is 3, `2 -1 ^` 2), and multiplies with `DBRA` on the low word of exponent-2 (`3 65538 ^` is 9) | the integer power: x^0 = 1, 0^n = 0 for n > 0, the low 32 bits of the power over all 32 bits of the exponent (square and multiply, at most 62 multiplications); a negative exponent gives the truncated value: 0, 1 for the base 1, 1 or -1 for the base -1 by the exponent's parity; 0^-n is error 3 "Divide by 0 error!" (user decision W2 Q2) | **F** | #641: only `^` with the exponent 0, a base 0, a negative exponent or an exponent above 65537 (test `rievallib-power-exponents`, `libs_emulator` cases `[fixed #641]` of `rwe_rievallib`) |
| rievallib init (rievallib.c: `_libinit`) | is `RTS`, as finit: a second run of the program with the library still loaded (from the editor) keeps the first run's PFRegisters block, freed at its end, its last error number and the address of its last indirection | init clears the block, the error number and the indirect address: a register before the run's own PFRegisters is error 2 "Register structure not found!", PFErrorType before the first PFEvaluate 0, PFIndirectAddr before the first indirection 0 | **F** | #1087: only a second run from the editor (D-019: no Blitz program can run twice with the library loaded; `libs_emulator` cases `[fixed #1087]` of `rwe_rievallib`, the scenario "second run") |

| rilinkedlistlib AddFirstListItem (rilinkedlistlib.c: `c_addfirstitem`) | writes the new node into offset 0 of the old first node, its next (v0.9 had prev there): the old first node loses its link to the second node, it and the new node point to each other, and NextListItem from the first node loops between the two | the new node becomes the old first node's prev | **F** | #724: every AddFirstListItem on a list with nodes (test `rilinkedlistlib-addfirst-next`, `libs_emulator` cases `[fixed #724]` of `rwe_rilinkedlistlib`) |

| risnowlib InitSnowDrop (risnowlib.c: `drophead`) | skips the drop lists' "last" field (`ADDQ.l #4,a0`): it stays 0 until the first LDoSnow, and an LDoSnowActivate before it appends its entries at address 0 and writes the end mark `$ABCD` there (with released flakes over the ExecBase pointer at 4) | "last" is the list's end mark, as for the kill lists | **F** | #908: only LDoSnowActivate before the first LDoSnow of a field (test `risnowlib-activate-before-dosnow`, `libs_emulator` cases `[fixed #908]` of `rwe_risnowlib`: the blocks until the first LDoSnow of each list) |
| risnowlib LDoSnowActivate (risnowlib.c: `dokilllist_c`) | appends the flakes it releases from the drop list's "last" on but stores the new end only into the kill list's "last": a second LDoSnowActivate before the next LDoSnow overwrites the flakes of the first, which are cleared in plane 1 and vanish without falling | the drop list's "last" is updated too | **F** | #909: only two or more LDoSnowActivate without an LDoSnow between them, the first releasing a flake (test `risnowlib-activate-last-not-updated`, `libs_emulator` cases `[fixed #909]` of `rwe_risnowlib`) |
| risnowlib init (risnowlib.c: `libinit_c`) | is empty: the list table keeps the pointers of the run before, so in a second run of a program from the editor (the library stays loaded, memlib has freed the blocks) a snow command before InitSnowDrop works on the freed lists | init clears the list table, as at the first load | **F** | #1135: only a second run from the editor (D-019: no Blitz program can run twice with the library loaded; `libs_emulator` cases `[fixed #1135]` of `rwe_risnowlib`, the scenario's second run) |
| risnowlib InitSnowDrop (risnowlib.c: `initsnowdrop_c`) | allocates a new block for the four lists and overwrites the pointers of the list number without freeing the old block: every repeated InitSnowDrop of a number loses numpoints × 64 + 40 bytes until the end of the program | the old block is freed first (memlib's `globfree`) | **F** | #911: only InitSnowDrop twice for a list number (test `risnowlib-initsnowdrop-no-free`, `libs_emulator` cases `[fixed #911]` of `rwe_risnowlib`: the memory the second InitSnowDrop of list 1 keeps) |
| risnowlib InitSnowDrop, LDoSnow, KillPoint, LDoSnowActivate (risnowlib.c: `initchk_c`, `fieldchk_c`, `killchk_c`) | find the snow field by the list number with `ASL.w #4` in a table of 4 × 16 bytes without a check: 4 and up, negative numbers or a non-zero upper word read and write behind or before the table (InitSnowDrop writes its four list pointers there); no error check area | with runtime error checking: "Snow list number must be 0 to 3"; LDoSnow, KillPoint and LDoSnowActivate also "Snow list needs InitSnowDrop" (a number without its field: the lists read at address 0), KillPoint "KillPoint outside the bitmap" (x outside 0–319, y outside the bitmap's rows); the release code unchanged | **F** | #913: only invalid list numbers or pixels with runtime error checking (test `risnowlib-listnum-unchecked`) |
| risnowlib LDoSnow (risnowlib.c: `_AsmDoSnow`) | takes the bit of a new flake from the random table as a whole byte; a blocked flake looks up its side moves with bit × 8 in the tables of 8 rows: bits 8–15 read the right-first table behind the left-first one (the wrong neighbours), higher ones the code behind the tables, and the flake jumps up to ±32 KB from its place, every frame again | the bit modulo 8 (as the bit operations take it) | **F** | #910: only random tables with bit bytes above 7 (test `risnowlib-bit-above-7-lookup`) |
| risnowlib LDoSnow, LDoSnowActivate, KillPoint (risnowlib.c: `_AsmDoSnow`, `unpause`, `killpoint_c`) | append to the drop and kill lists without counting: LDoSnow one new flake per call, LDoSnowActivate the flakes it releases, KillPoint one entry per pixel; more than the numpoints × 4 entries of a list run into the next list and behind the block | a list takes at most numpoints × 4 − 1 entries: no new flake into a full drop list, stopped snow stays when it is full, KillPoint does nothing (0) when its list is full | **F** | #914: only fields whose lists fill up (flakes that pile up without stopping; test `risnowlib-lists-unbounded`) |

| ripacklib UnPackPBM (ripacklib.c: `unpackpbm_c`) | tests the source and destination limits after the access and signed (`CMP.l`/`BLT`): reads the byte at source+length, writes one byte at dest+destlength, reads the bytes of a literal run past the source's end | both limits tested before each access, unsigned; a literal run stops at the source's end | **F** | #778: data that unpack to more than destlength bytes or end inside a literal run (test `ripacklib-unpackpbm-limits`; `libs_emulator` cases `[fixed #778]` of `rwe_ripacklib`); rishapeslib's LELoadShapes, which unpacks a compressed shape with UnPackPBM into a block of the shape's size, no longer writes the byte behind that block for data that unpack to more |
| ripacklib LoadIFF (ripacklib.c: `ilbmload_c`) | loads the file into the first free bank of RIAmosFuncLib from bank 51 on (`MOVEQ #51,d0 : NextBank`): with RIAmosFuncLib's default of 50 banks there is none, LoadIFF returns 0 and loads nothing unless the program raised the number with BankLimit | the first free bank from bank 0 on, as RWE's V2.5 of the library (`MOVEQ #0,D0 : ALibJsr NextBank,0`) | **F** | #770: every LoadIFF of a program that does not raise BankLimit above 51 (test `ripacklib-loadiff-bank-51`; `libs_emulator` case `[fixed #770]` of `rwe_ripacklib`); a program that keeps its own data in banks 0 to 50 sees the lowest free one used and erased again during the call |

| ripacklib UnpackIFF (ripacklib.c: `unpackiff_c`) | checks the BMHD's nPlanes only against the bitmap's depth: 0 passes, the depth becomes 255 and 256 plane pointers are copied into the table of 8, over the unpacker's variables and the library's code behind it (a crash at the next call) | nPlanes 0 is refused (0) before the table is written | **F** | #777: an ILBM whose BMHD says 0 planes, through UnpackIFF, ILBMGrab, LoadIFF and rianimlib's RIAnimInit (test `ripacklib-unpackiff-zero-planes`) |
| ripacklib UnpackIFF (ripacklib.c: `unpackiff_c`) | takes the bytes of a row as width/8 (`ASR.w #3`): ILBM pads its rows to words, so for a width that is no multiple of 16 every row after the first is read out of step (and the last pixels of a width that is no multiple of 8 are missing) | the word-aligned row, `((width + 15) / 16) * 2` bytes, also for the test against the bitmap's bytes per row | **F** | #775: pictures whose width is no multiple of 16, through UnpackIFF, ILBMGrab, LoadIFF and rianimlib's RIAnimInit (test `ripacklib-unpackiff-row-width`; `libs_emulator` cases `[fixed #775]` of `rwe_ripacklib`); a picture whose padded row is one byte wider than the bitmap's row is now refused |

| ripacklib UnpackIFF (ripacklib.c: `unpackiff_c`) | writes every literal or repeat run completely and only then counts it against the row: a run longer than the rest of the row is written into the next row of the plane, in the last row behind the plane | the run is cut at the end of the row, the source still skips the whole run (as ilbmifflib, #191) | **F** | #776: corrupt BODY data or an encoder whose runs cross rows (test `ripacklib-unpackiff-run-past-row`; `libs_emulator` case `[fixed #776]` of `rwe_ripacklib`) |
