| rilesdebuglib ProcessString (rilesdebuglib.c: `processstring_c`) | takes the 0 behind a `%` at the end of the text as an entry's name and reads on behind the text | the text ends there | **F** | #720: texts that end in `%` (the debugger's menu file lines, D-006; test `rilesdebuglib-processstring-trailing-percent`) |

| rilesdebuglib FPrintDetoke (rilesdebuglib.c: `draw_char`, `fprintdetoke_c`) | tests only the high byte of the FInverse word (FInverse 1 inverts FPrint only); its inverse space marker sets and clears that byte, which turns FInverse $100 off | the word is tested, as by FPrint; the marker keeps it | **F** | #706: FInverse with a low byte only, and printing behind the marker (test `rilesdebuglib-finverse-detoke`, `libs_emulator` cases `[fixed #706]` of `rwe_rilesdebuglib`) |
| rilesdebuglib FPrintDetoke (rilesdebuglib.c: `draw_char`, `fprintdetoke_c`) | with inverse printing and tokenise, draws a token's character into plane 1 with the glyph offset of d0, whose low byte the inverse drawing overwrote with the last glyph byte: another character | plane 1 gets the character's own glyph | **F** | #709: token names with FInverse on and tokenise (test `rilesdebuglib-detoke-plane1-glyph`, `libs_emulator` case `[fixed #709]` of `rwe_rilesdebuglib`; the first run's planes in the `[fixed #706]` cases) |

| rilesdebuglib AddProcessPtr, init (rilesdebuglib.c: `addprocessptr_c`, `addprocessptrchk_c`, `libinit_c`) | appends to the table of 20 entries without a count: the 21st goes over the end mark and the bytes behind the table (the code of D_InitParse; the C build: other data); init leaves the entries of the run before | at most 20 entries, a 21st is ignored; with runtime error checking "AddProcessPtr table holds at most 20 entries"; init clears the 20 entries | **F** | #700: programs that add more than 20 entries, and a second run from the editor (tests `rilesdebuglib-addprocessptr-no-limit`, `rilesdebuglib-addprocessptr-full-check`, runtime error checking; `libs_emulator` cases `[fixed #700]` of `rwe_rilesdebuglib`) |

## 4. deflibs (AmigaOS call declarations)

| Library/routine (file:line) | Original | C version | Kind | Reason / observable? |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| init of all libraries/resources (libs/deflibs/deflib.h:16) | `BEQ init+6` jumps into the middle of the `LEA`: `ORI.b #0,(a0)+` with arbitrary a0, then OpenLibrary with the a1 left by the failure, forever | the base or 0 (no retry); the finit of a library closes it only if the base is not 0 | **F** | #44: a program using a call of a missing library no longer hangs at startup; it runs with base 0, a call of that library crashes (test `deflibs-init-endless-retry`, Kickstart 1.3 without gadtools.library) |
| init/finit in general ([deflibs.md](deflibs.md)) | destroys a6, `JMP CloseLibrary` | GCC saves a6, `JSR` | **B** (register preservation) | not observable |
| Resources (deflib.h, e.g. battclocklib.c:42) | `LEA` points 6 bytes into the name: `OpenResource` always fails, the init hangs (#44) | opens the name itself | **F** | #45: a program using a call of ciaa, ciab, disk, misc, potgo, battclock, battmem or card no longer hangs at startup (test `deflibs-resource-name-skew`) |
| exec `CachePreDMA_`, `CachePostDMA_` (execlib.c:141) | `flags` declared in d1, exec reads d0 | d0 (`exec_lib.fd`); a declaration difference, listed in `libs/deflibs/declarations-intended.txt` | **F** | #959: exec gets the given flags; before, only by chance at the statement level (the flags evaluated last into d0), inside an expression d0 held another value (test `deflibs-cachedma-flags-d1`) |

## 5. Compiler blitz2-c (`programs/blitz2`)

Produces byte-identical programs (test corpus and CD reference run), hence only
internal deviations; except the fixed original bugs (kind **F**), which
change the generated code of the programs they concern. Conventions: `-fbaserel` (globals a4-relative,
`GLUE_LOAD_A4` in all entry points from outside, [README](../blitz2/bb2-readme.md)), assembler only in
`programs/blitz2/src/glue*.c`, registers with persistent state as globals
(`srcptr`; `numval`/`numfloat` local to libcall.c).

| Place (file:line) | Original | C version | Kind | observable? |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| errstack/errcont (asmlibs.c:1427, compile.c:184, data.c:1460, data.c:1830, main.c:1354, parse.c:216, parse.c:305, stmts.c:1189, stmts.c:1634) | error return is not reset | `errjmp = olderr` | **T** | stack trick (a7/errcont) only with longjmp in C; no |
| Stack tricks `addq #4,a7` / `move.l passstack,a7` (asmlibs.c:403, :467, asmlibs.h:69, compile.c:1108, tokens1.c:840, tokens1.c:953) | return past the caller | return value / longjmp | **T** | no |
| Placeholder longs (compile.c:829, stmts.c:432, tokens1.c:256, :545, :673, :1158, tokens2.c:10) | poke register leftovers as placeholders | 0 or the recognisable value | **T** | are always overwritten (except nomemleft); no |
| Register results (eval.h:236, eval.c:126, libcall.h:19, compile.c:1385, tokens1.c:343, tokens2.c:81, parse.c:1163) | results in d2/d3/d6/CCR, values from the caller's stack | globals / parameters / return values | **T** | no |
| Constant folding (eval.h:171, eval.c:824, libcall.c:1182) | executes generated code with the compiler registers | `callregs` with the relevant registers | **T** | no |
| Range/overflow checks (eval.c:486, data.c:1693, parse.c:351, compile.c:198) | without check (reading/writing beyond tables, DIVU by 0) | C checks and does nothing | **K** | only in cases that according to the comment do not occur |
| pushprec (eval.c:944) | stores operator/precedence without bounds check; "Expression too complex" only in `popprec`, after writing past the end of the stack | checks before writing and reports `pserr` immediately | **K** | same message; the only difference is that the original first writes beyond `precstackf` (only with very deeply nested expressions) |
| savefile HUNK_SYMBOL (parse.c:269) | writes the name length rounded up to longwords directly from the label node: **reads** up to 3 bytes behind the allocation and writes them as padding bytes into the executable | copy padded with zeros (padding bytes 0) | **K** | only with "Create Debug Info": the padding bytes in HUNK_SYMBOL (original random, C always 0); otherwise no |
| libcall.c:256, libcall.c:1438 | makeint/comparison with leftovers in the upper byte | FALSE / defined comparison | **T** | no |
| parse.c:544, parse.h:75, compile.h:32 | err without a set return | message + `exit(RETURN_FAIL)` | **T** | only with internal errors |
| parse.c:825 | `findlast` (dead code) | walks the list | **T** | no |
| stmts.c:1664 | flushc after the register restore | before it | **T** | no |
| Start (main.c:31, :1072, :1096, :1134, :1141) | own Workbench/CLI start, opens/closes dos.library | libnix start (DOSBase, WBStartup, argv) | **T** | command line is split by libnix (quotes) |
| main.c:1529 | SuperState/movec/UserState | `Supervisor()` | **T** | no |
| calcvbr (main.c: `calcvbr`) | the VBR is read only from the 68020 on: on a 68010 the trap vectors of a program run from the editor (directtrap, debugger) go into the table at 0 | read from the 68010 on (as the libraries, section 2) | **F** | #42: only on a 68010 with a moved vector table; not covered by a test (the test `multi-68010-vbr` runs an executable) |
| runtheprog (main.c: `runtheprog`), setint2 (stmts.c: `setint2`) | a program run from the editor: the token list goes into the CPU's TRAP #1 vector and `firsttype` into the TRAP #2 vector as a mailbox (debuglib reads the token list there and puts its handlers over both), the TRAP #15 vector gets `directtrap` and keeps it after the run; a SetInt routine compiled with runtime error checking starts with TRAP #2, its parameter, then the call of runerrlib's gosuper | the token list goes into the program process's tc_UserData (`firsttype` nowhere: nothing reads it), the TRAP #15 vector is put back when the program ends (or is stopped with PANIC); gosuper is called before the TRAP #2 | **F** | #1069 (row of section 2): the libraries take the traps through the task's trap handler, so the vectors must stay the system's, and a SetInt routine needs gosuper's vectors for its TRAP #2. The executables with a SetInt routine and runtime error checking change (the call moves 6 bytes forward); the skip of a routine (debugger stopped, or after its runtime error) now passes gosuper too, so runerrlib's stack check count stays balanced |
| savefile HUNK_RELOC32 (parse.c:243) | all relocations of the code hunk in one group (count, hunk 0, offsets): with more than 65535 the LoadSeg of AmigaOS V36+ refuses the executable ("file is not executable"; Kickstart 1.3 loads it) | groups of at most 65535 offsets in the block | **F** | #943: the executable differs only with more than 65535 relocations (8 bytes more per further group); the resident writer (stmts.c) is unchanged (residents are loaded by blitz2 itself). Test `compiler-reloc32-one-block` |
| stamigalib, amigalib (stmts.c:629, eval.c:2004) | Amiga library call with a parameter in a4 or a5 (graphics LockLayerRom_, UnlockLayerRom_, AttemptLockLayerRom_, workbench AddAppIconA_, exec Supervisor_; before #960 Supervisor_ could not be compiled at all): the parameters are popped into their registers before `move.l off(a5),a6`, a4/a5 not saved - with a5 a6 is garbage (crash at the jsr), after the call a4/a5 hold the parameters instead of the variable bases | `movem.l a4/a5,-(a7)` before the parameters, the library base before the pops, `movem.l (a7)+,a4/a5` after the jsr | **F** | #958: only calls with an a4/a5 parameter get other code (8 bytes more); test `compiler-amigalib-a4a5` |
| doincbin (tokens1.c:824) | IncBin of a directory: "Can't open Include" with the lock left behind; a read error: "Error Reading File" with the file left open; Examine unchecked (an old content of macrobuff counts as the FileInfoBlock) | the lock is released and the file closed before the error; a failed Examine is "Can't open Include" | **F** | #38: the same errors, nothing left in use (tests `compiler-incbin-leaks`, `compiler-incbin-leaks-readerr`) |
| fixconsts (stmts.c:1105) | the next of the last constant of a resident is left as loaded: in a resident whose last constant is followed by other labels (Create Resident, `resconsts`; AmiBlitz3's `all.res`) it points to the end of the hunk, and every compile's `freelabels` (from `lastrescon`) frees what lies behind the hunk (alerts 8100 0005 / 0100 000F, depending on that memory) | next of the last constant 0, as AmiBlitz3's fixconsts (`CLR.l (a2)`, `AmiBlitz/AmiBlitz3@ea8c25c7`) | **F** | #34: no FreeMem behind the resident; residents written by Create Resident are unchanged (test `compiler-resconsts-last-next`) |
| blitz2's exit, fail2b (main.c:527) | `macrobuff`, allocated at the start with the Macro Buffer size of then (8192), is freed with the current `macrolen`: an .xtra loaded later (Macro Buffer of the Compiler Options) changes it - a smaller size leaves the rest lost, a larger one (AmiBlitz3's default 40000) corrupts exec's free list (alert 8100 0005 at the exit) | freed with the size of the allocation (`macrobuffsize`) | **F** | #336: only blitz2/blitz2-ab3 change, no generated code; AmiBlitz3 (`AmiBlitz/AmiBlitz3@c7fc6f60`) still frees with the current size (tests `compiler-macrobuff-freemem`, `compiler-macrobuff-freemem-large`, `ab3_cli` case `macrobuf_xtra`) |
| makemacbuff (stmts.c:784), called by loadxtra and optsdone | the Macro Buffer option has no effect: `macrobuff` keeps its 8192 bytes of the start, a macro's text is limited to 8192 bytes whatever the option says | where the other option-sized buffers follow the options (`makeiallox` in loadxtra and optsdone, outside of a compile), `macrobuff` is reallocated when `macrolen` differs from its limit: a macro's text is limited to `macrolen` ("Macro too Big"), at least 8192 bytes are allocated (doincbin's FileInfoBlock and the unchecked macro parameters, #4, use the buffer too); if the allocation fails the old buffer and its limit stay | **F** | #336: a source with a larger Macro Buffer compiles macros of more than 8192 bytes, a smaller one rejects macros between it and 8192 bytes; with the default 8192 nothing changes (tests `compiler-macrobuff-size`, macrobuf=16384, `compiler-macrobuff-size-small`, macrobuf=1024). A Macro Buffer over 65535 reaches the kept `BUG(orig)` of the macro text length (a word, tokens1.c domacro) |
| domacro (tokens1.c:945) | the node of a new macro (addhere, AllocMem without MEMF_CLEAR) gets only its use count; text pointer and length are written at End Macro: an error before it ("Macro too Big", "Macro without End Macro") ends the compile with the node half-built, and freemacs (errcont freeemup) frees a text with the pointer and length the memory held - on Kickstart 3.x an alert (0100 000F, 8100 0005) that waits for the mouse, the error never shown; with a 0 in the length word nothing happens | text pointer and length cleared after addhere: the half-built macro has no text | **F** | #965: only the error path changes, no generated code (tests `compiler-macro-uninit-free`, `compiler-macro-uninit-free-noend`, with inject fill=a5) |
| getchar `.cmake` (parse.c:432) | every character outside a false CNIF block is compared with `~`, the start of the constant maker `~expr~`, also inside a string literal: a `~` in a `Dc.b`, `Print`, `#"…"` or `Data$` string gives "Illegal Constant Expression", a string with a valid `~expr~` gets its value put in (`"a~1+2~b"` is `a3b`) | `~` starts a constant maker only outside quotes (`quoteflag`); inside a string literal it is an ordinary character, nothing is computed inside strings | **F** | #966: BB2 and AB3 mode; a program without `~` in a string literal compiles to the same bytes, `~expr~` outside strings is unchanged (test `compiler-cmake-tilde-in-string`, `ab3_cli`/`ab3_cli_ref` case `tilde_string`) |
| zeropc: doeven4, doeven8, alignpc/doeven, dods (tokens1.c:582), pokewd (eval.c:210); litstring (eval.c:2237), dodata (tokens1.c:751) | `Even`, `Even4`, `Even8`, the word alignment of `Dc.w/.l`, `Dcb.w/.l` and of inline assembler instructions (alignpc), the alignment pokewd does before every word of code or data (an odd `Dc.b`/`IncBin` followed directly by code), and `Ds` move `pc` only: the skipped pad bytes and the reserved storage hold what the object buffer held there - with runtime error checking on the debug record undodebug withdrew for the label or directive (trap #1, line item address, column), with Compile/Run the bytes of the previous compile of the session. A string literal (litstring, data1) and a `Data$` string (dodata, data2) of odd length round their pointer up to even, the pad byte is not written either: with Compile/Run it holds the previous compile's byte | the skipped bytes are written as 0, the way pokebyte writes (bounded by `libat`, `nomemleft` past it, `bigpc`); the pad byte of a string literal or `Data$` string as 0 with pokedata1b/pokedata2b (bounded like the string's bytes) | **F** | #969: BB2 and AB3 mode; executables differ only in those bytes, with Create Executable only with runtime error checking on (otherwise the buffer is cleared); of the CD's sources only QUIKASM.BB2 (runtime error checking on, `Ds` behind labels; `tests/data/blitz2-cd.tsv` holds the port's hash, with a note). The pads of string literals and `Data$` strings change only the program in memory of Compile/Run (Create Executable compiles into a cleared buffer: executables unchanged). (tests `compiler-even-pad-byte`, `compiler-even-pad-byte-code`, `compiler-data-pad-byte`) |
| Create Resident: writeitem, writeoffs, restypes, resmacs, resconsts (stmts.c:890); addhere (parse.c:882), domacro (tokens1.c:1048) | every record is written with its length rounded up to even: for an odd one (constant, NewType field, type, macro, macro text) the byte behind its allocation (addhere and domacro allocate exactly the length, MEMF_PUBLIC). A constant's data and procnum keep what the allocation held (handleconst sets only refs and the value; `#c` without `=` not even the value), a field's type keeps the low word of the type's address (writeoffs replaces the high word by the type number), a type's next is the pointer to the next type in memory. The loaders never read these bytes, but the same source gives a different file depending on the memory | a 0 behind an odd record, the type number alone in a field's type (low word 0), next 0 in a type record; the nodes (addhere) and the macro text allocated with MEMF_CLEAR (a constant without `=` is 0) | **F** | #970: BB2 and AB3 mode; residents written by Create Resident differ in those bytes (and are reproducible), loading is unchanged. Executables differ only for sources that read a node field nobody set: `#c` without `=` (value 0); a NewType field of the type being defined, which read the type's size, is refused (#973). (test `compiler-resident-stale-bytes`, with inject guard fill=a5) |
| handleconst (compile.c:1332, blitz2.s:3065); addhere (parse.c:882) | a constant defined without a value (a line `#c`, no `=`) is accepted but its value is never set: the node comes from addhere without MEMF_CLEAR, so `#c` holds what the memory held (`CNIF #c` is true for any non-zero value; a program can compile different code from run to run). `#c` for a constant that has a value keeps it | a constant without an explicitly assigned value is 0 (the node is allocated cleared, #970), as AmiBlitz3 gives in practice (same `handleconst`, label nodes from a pool created with MEMF_CLEAR); `CNIF #c` is false | **F** | #972: BB2 and AB3 mode; executables differ only for sources that use such a constant. (tests `compiler-const-no-value` with inject fill=a5, `ab3_cli_const_novalue`) |

## 5a. Editor ted-c (TED 1.1, `programs/ted`)

The reference is the vasm build of `ted.s` ([`../ted/reference.md`](../ted/reference.md)).
Separate list of the TED deviations: [`../ted/deviations.md`](../ted/deviations.md)
(continuously extended there); here the rows that concern the shared
file requester.

| Place (file:line) | Original | C version | Kind | observable? |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| `flink` (ted.s:7953; programs/ted/src/filereq.c) | the embedded requester hunk `filereq` relocates itself at runtime on start (from `outside`) (`add.l basis` on 312 longwords in its own code hunk, without cache flush; a second run would relocate twice – BUG(orig), #360) | dropped: the requester is C code (`shared/filereq/filereq_core.c`), its pointers are relocated by LoadSeg | **T** | technically unavoidable: no embedded hunk any more, nothing to relocate. No (except the double relocation, which never happens in the original) |
| Requester data block (programs/ted/src/filereq.c with `shared/filereq/filereq_core.c`) | TED's copy of `filereq` differs from the one in freqlib in 7 bytes (padding bytes behind gadget texts, e.g. `"OK",0,$06` instead of `$00`, and remainder at the hunk end) | data block of the freqlib version | **T** | one source for both programs (user decision "Variante C"); the bytes are never read. No |
| Requester layout, setheight, `nw.MaxHeight` (programs/ted/src/filereq.c with `shared/filereq/filereq_core.c`) | TED's embedded requester has the bug of freqlib (#160, section 3): more list lines than its 64 line gadgets on a screen of 645 lines or more or in a window enlarged to 605 or more, written behind its data block into TED | at most 64 list lines, the window at most 597 high (the same code as freqlib) | **F** | #160: in TED on a screen of 645 lines or more (TED's screen has the size of the Workbench and must be planar, so not in the RTG variant of the test emulator) or in a window enlarged to 605 lines or more; covered by freqlib's test `freqlib-64-lines` (the same code) |
| Requester gadget "CD" (programs/ted/src/filereq.c with `shared/filereq/filereq_core.c`) | TED's embedded requester has the bug of freqlib (#345, section 3): CD clears the high word of the path gadget's `UndoBuffer`, and Intuition then copies the path into the first 64 KB when the gadget is activated | clears `BufferPos` and `DispPos` (the same code as freqlib) | **F** | #345: in TED after CD in the file requester; covered by freqlib's test `freqlib-cd-undobuffer` (the same code) |
| Requester libraries (programs/ted/src/filereq.c with `shared/filereq/filereq_core.c`) | TED's embedded requester has the bug of freqlib (#154): it opens dos, intuition and graphics at every call and never closes them | closes them at the end of every call (the same code as freqlib) | **F** | #154: the open counts of the three libraries no longer rise with every file requester of TED; covered by freqlib's test `freqlib-libs-not-closed` (the same code) |
| Requester volume list (programs/ted/src/filereq.c with `shared/filereq/filereq_core.c`) | TED's embedded requester has the bug of freqlib (#156): DRIVES walks the DOS list without locking it | walks it locked for reading (the same code as freqlib) | **F** | #156: only while another process changes or holds the DOS list; covered by freqlib's test `freqlib-doslist-unlocked` (the same code) |
| Requester path of the current directory (programs/ted/src/filereq.c with `shared/filereq/filereq_core.c`) | TED's embedded requester has the bug of freqlib (#158): PARENT with an empty path and CD leave the `ParentDir` locks of the path behind | unlocks them (the same code as freqlib) | **F** | #158: the parent directories are no longer in use after TED's file requester; covered by freqlib's test `freqlib-parentdir-leak` (the same code) |
| Requester gadget "CD", end of TED (programs/ted/src/filereq.c `filereq_finit`, main.c `outside_run`, with `shared/filereq/filereq_core.c`) | TED's embedded requester has the bug of freqlib (#157): CD makes the path TED's current directory and unlocks the old one (the shell's, or blitz2's start directory) | CD keeps the old directory; the end of TED (`fail2`, every way out, also under blitz2) makes it current again and unlocks the requester's lock (the same code as freqlib) | **F** | #157: after CD in TED's file requester the directory TED (or blitz2) was started in is not unlocked and is current again when TED ends; while TED runs, loads and compiles still go to the path's directory; TED bug-orig test `ted-freq-cd-start-lock` |
| Requester list nodes (programs/ted/src/filereq.c with `shared/filereq/filereq_core.c`) | TED's embedded requester has the bug of freqlib (#159): 40-byte list nodes, overrun by names of more than 34 characters, AllocMem not checked | nodes of the size of the name, an entry without memory left out (the same code as freqlib) | **F** | #159: in TED's file requester on a file system with long names, or without memory; covered by freqlib's tests `freqlib-node-overrun` and `freqlib-node-allocfail` (the same code) |
| Requester names and paths (programs/ted/src/filereq.c with `shared/filereq/filereq_core.c`) | TED's embedded requester has the bug of freqlib (#1042, section 3): names and paths are copied into its 160-byte path and 64-byte file buffers without a limit; a file name of 64 or more characters, clicked and taken with OK, makes the copy run on without end | a name or path that does not fit (159 / 63 characters) is refused with "Path too long" / "Name too long" in the screen title bar, the buffers keep their contents (the same code as freqlib); TED's buffers (192 / 64 bytes) take what the requester's own take | **F** | #1042: in TED's file requester with names of 64 or more characters or long paths; covered by freqlib's tests `freqlib-file-name-unbounded`, `regression/freqlib-names-refused` and `regression/freqlib-names-limits` (the same code) |

## 6. Error checks outside the error check area

A check function in the normal code is kept in every program, also
without the debugger (the behaviour is the same: the thunks in the check
area call it). Top-level assembler that switches the section is
invisible to GCC: a `BLITZ_ERRCHK` C function behind it can get no
section directive and land in the normal code. The assembler routines
of `blitzlib.h` (`BLITZ_ERRCHK_THUNK`, `BLITZ_ERRCHK_MESSAGE`,
`BLITZ_ERRCHK_AB`, the thunks of the normal code ...) are therefore C
functions whose body is the assembler text, and GCC switches the section
itself (#1056). So is the libraries' own assembler (`BLITZ_ERRCHK_ASM`,
`BLITZ_ASM_ROUTINE`, `BLITZ_ASM_BLOCK`), and the build step of every
library refuses a section directive in the assembler of its sources
(#1057). `blitzlib check` reports a check thunk in the error check area
whose C function is in the normal code, in the build step of every
library (`docs/development.md`, "Checks in the build"). Every
`BLITZ_ERRCHK` function and message of the acidlibs and the RWE userlibs
is in the error check area (checked against the symbols of all `.linked`
files for #1056).

## 7. State

All command libraries are ported (c4a736d); the tables also contain
scrolllib, 2dlib, polylib (S12) and gameiolib, blitzkeyslib, animlib,
blitzcoplib, displaylib (S13); dhandlerlib (lib 1), the last original
binary in acidlibs, followed on 2026-10-29.

## Summary

* **B – user decision:** the a2 convention (about 20 libraries,
  never observable) and register preservation "at least like the original"
  (additionally preserved registers are never results).
* **T – technically unavoidable:** undefined register leftovers and
  OS returns (blitlib BlitColl, freqlib, cliargslib CCR, windowslib
  zcursor, ilbmifflib a0), memory layout with overflows and
  invalid indices (2dlib, polylib, displaylib, crunchlib), stack contents
  with error jumps via JSR (displaylib) and other stack questions
  (crunchlib, arrayslib a4/a5), timing (shapetrixlib XFlip) and in the
  compiler all stack tricks and placeholders. Observable at most in
  error cases, with overflows or corrupted data.
* **K – silent corrections (existing):** traps in normal code instead of a jump
  into the cut-off error check area (inputoutputlib, fileiolib,
  palettelib, windowslib, brexxlib), return instead of crash
  or continuing error chain or running into the message text (arexxlib,
  memlib err1/locfree, runerrlib, fontlib, palettelib, seriallib noser,
  utillib CreateMsgPort), preserved a3 (linklistlib, medlib, seriallib,
  windowslib, blitzoslib), the title length not written into the tag list
  (windowslib WindowTags &TagList) and word coordinates
  (blitzoslib), in the compiler some range checks (pushprec, savefile).
  Observable mostly only in error cases without debugger or with a
  returning trap; visible in a normal run are seriallib
  ReadSerial in string expressions and WindowTags &TagList (memory behind
  the tag list).
* **F – fixed original bugs (intended):** the rows of kind **F** in the
  tables above, each with its issue and test; mostly error routines and
  their messages moved out of the error check area, overruns, leaks and
  wrong compares. They change the output of the affected cases on
  purpose.
